ParlaMint-PL_2019-03-13-sejm-78-1.u7	Thank you, Member. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear five minutes' statements on behalf of clubs and circles. I'm opening a discussion. Mr Andrzej Noblet, Parliamentary Club Law and Justice, will be the first to speak.
ParlaMint-PL_2019-03-13-sejm-78-1.u8	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour to present the position of the Parliamentary Club Law and Justice towards the government draft law on supporting scientific activities from the Polish Science Fund, print no. 3200. In line with the Responsible Development Strategy adopted by the Council of Ministers on 14 February 2017, the development of science is an important element contributing to an increase in the level of competitiveness of national actors. This strategy includes the internationalisation and opening of Polish universities to innovation and ensuring inter alia. a legal environment conducive to innovation and competitiveness of research results by universities and research entities. Scientific activities supported by the Polish Science Fund should focus not only on research and development, but also on commercialisation of their results in the form of innovative products which will be able to compete on the Polish and global market. The solutions adopted in the draft law implement the strategy's objectives both in terms of increasing funding for scientific activities and in terms of supporting the emergence and development of innovative ways of organising R &amp; D activities. One of the flagship projects mentioned in the strategy are the Biotechnology Development Centre and Polish medical devices. The resources of the fund will first be allocated to the broad-based biotechnology area. The National Science Centre provides financial support for projects only in the field of basic research, and the National Research and Development Centre finances almost exclusively industrial research and development. The analysis of the existing legal situation shows that there is no way to implement the solution provided for in the proposed law based on available legal tools and institutions creating a higher education and science system. The introduction of the proposed solutions in this project is linked to the need to create a new financing and commercialisation mechanism for research and development. An important feature of the proposed solution will be the provision of a stable financing system, which will be available in the long term for 5 and 10 years. The draft law aims to create the basis and mechanism for financing scientific activities carried out in a revised form so-called. a virtual research institute, in short WIB, which will be a form of organisation of selected, internationally competitive research teams carrying out scientific activities with high potential for socio-economic applications. WIB will not have legal personality, nor will it be an employer for researchers forming part of the research team. WIB funding fund will be established in Bank Gospodarstwa Krajowego. It will be separate from the state budget and will be able to finance these objectives over the long term. The main source of funding for the Fund is expected to be the amount of PLN 500 million that it will receive as a result of the reduction in the statutory fund of Bank Gospodarstwa Krajowego. The aim of the Fund is to finance scientific activities, which are particularly important for the implementation of the country's development strategy. The draft law introduces the design and financing of WIB's scientific activities based on contracts between the parties involved. The draft law sets the maximum amount of funding to finance research teams carrying out research activities under the WIB. [[♪ The bell ♪]] The Minister shall decide how the funds are to be spent. The bill does not require the presentation to the competent authorities...
ParlaMint-PL_2019-03-13-sejm-78-1.u9	Thank you, sir.
ParlaMint-PL_2019-03-13-sejm-78-1.u10	...and the institutions of the European Union. At the same time, on behalf of the club, I make four amendments to this project.
ParlaMint-PL_2019-03-13-sejm-78-1.u11	Thank you. Ladies and gentlemen, welcome children and young people from the Youth Sociotherapy Centre of Gniezno sitting at the gallery. [[Applause]] We're very pleased that you're in the Seym. Now, please take the floor of Mr Adam Korol, PO-KO. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2019-03-13-sejm-78-1.u12	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, the Civic Platform - Coalition I am pleased to present an opinion on the government draft law on the creation of the Polish Science Fund. Like most draft laws proposed by Law and Justice, the draft law on the Polish Science Fund is an underdeveloped document. It concerns the promotion of commercialisation of research results through the creation of a new research funding mechanism - the Polish Science Fund in Bank Gospodarstwa Krajowego through the innovative formula of virtual research institutes in Poland. The only plus of this idea is to increase the expenditure on scientific activities, which will translate into modernising entrepreneurship. However, the proposed solutions raise so important doubts that even the Sejm Analysis Office suggests that the project should be discussed in depth. It may be more effective to modify the activities of existing entities such as the National Centre for Research and Development or the National Science Centre. Creating new solutions from scratch is less rational than exploiting existing potential. The project aims to centralise decision-making, i.e. the area of action and the amount of funding to be decided by policies, specifically - the Minister for Science and Higher Education, without trying to consult the scientific community or entrepreneurs. What, then, will be the basis for decisions on what and to what extent the funds will be granted? Only on the basis of the Minister's will. Moreover, for incomprehensible reasons, the project places the Polish Centre for Technology Development in a privileged position, which can only be appointed by the Minister to manage the project without competition. Other centres must apply for these functions in the tender contest mode. The question of funding the fund. For this purpose 500 million PLN is to be allocated once from the statutory fund of Bank Gospodarstwa Krajowego. This amount is intended to last for 10 years, which means little financial capacity of the fund compared to e.g. the budget of the National Centre for Research and Development, which this year will receive a grant of PLN 1300 million. So why create a fund that will not have financial opportunities in practice? Here's another thing. The project does not define the terms and conditions of cooperation with entrepreneurs, although it stresses the need to commercialise research results. The project lacks any concrete, even specific forms of financial support for companies willing to implement the proposed solutions. In the end, the project lacks decisions on intellectual property or on the principles of the use of research infrastructures of research institutes. In conclusion, the bill in its current form is likely to become a propaganda tool of Minister Gowin in the election campaign. It is therefore a sanctioned distribution of PLN 500 million without any inspection and consultation of experts. In view of the substantive shortcomings presented in the project, we will request that it be rejected in the proposed form. Thank you. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2019-03-13-sejm-78-1.u14	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! I present the position of the Kukiz club15 in relation to the draft law on supporting scientific activities from the Polish Science Fund, print no. 3200. The justification already put forward by your Law and Justice Member should be a sufficient argument to reject this bill. Nevertheless, I will present a few reflections on this subject in order to justify such a position. First, the title of this law is already false: about supporting scientific activities from the Polish Science Fund. There is nothing here or there is little about supporting Polish science, but it is about redistribution of funds allocated to it by a body dependent on the Ministry of Science and Higher Education. If the government really wanted to care about Polish science, it would apply a simple and much less expensive solution. It would simply allocate 500 million to existing entities, whether the NCN or the National Centre for Research and Development, possibly also already introduced the Research Network of Łukasiewicz. However, such a solution would reduce the decision-making and distribution possibilities resulting from the management of these measures. It would also be acceptable to argue for such an institution if the drafters of the law pointed out that the current channels for the transfer of funds to science produce such pathologies, such as the one revealed in the last days, when it turned out that the relative of the lady, who thought it was 6,000. zł works only idiots or thieves, is involved in extortion of several million PLN from NCBR. But the creators of this project, this legislation, do not say anything about such a solution, because they know that under their rule there may be no crime, but certainly there is scientific pathology when funds are granted for research supposedly from history, whose workshop is based on dreams as a source of facts, which is used by Barbara Engelking in her publications of slanderous Poles. The draft law, both substantively and legally, was massacred by the authors of the opinion from the Office of Sejm Analysis and there is nothing to offend them for this opinion. It is not only true, but it should also be a source of reflection on not only improving the project but even rejecting it. The draft law must be accused of a clear centralisation, because the area of scientific activity, the amount of funds, the period of their spending or the choice of the managing body will be decided by the Minister responsible for higher education and science. Where's the famous deregulation and decentralisation? Another charge that needs to be raised is that there is no solution on issues such as: the role of parent institutions of entities forming part of a virtual research institute. How will their research infrastructure be used? The project also ignores intellectual and industrial property issues. In theory, it could be assumed that these cases can be settled on the basis of existing legislation. Unfortunately, as the Higher Education Act shows, called 2.0 - and this is indeed a good name - this cannot be accepted. By way of proof, I will refer to the examination of the final work of postgraduate studies, which is very important from the point of view of the competences and powers acquired by their graduates. It turns out that Law 2.0 does not impose such an obligation on the university. This was the answer I received from the Ministry of Science and Higher Education when it turned out that postgraduate work could not be checked directly within the framework of a single anti-plagiarism system. The Ministry stated that the newly adopted law on higher education and the law on copyright should be amended. Of course, responsible universities must find a solution in this case, and they do it: either they buy an additional anti-plagiarism program or they celebrate the system as part of a single anti-plagiarism system. I will add, by the way, on the basis of this legislation, but also other legislation in this area, that it is evident proof for the Ministry of Poland, in which every minister wants to have some piece of cake to split to make himself feel important. This law also proves the thesis that in the government of Prime Minister Mateusz Morawiecki another group creates a backup for a soft landing after a possible electoral failure. However, these countess may turn out to be flaming, because [[♪ The bell ♪]] possible Polish rulers, who will win the election, can...

Thanks to the fact that the Polish Science Fund will be created, this variety of possibilities for carrying out research projects is increasing. The Polish Science Fund, due to its long-term investment in science, will focus on the research that they have, say, an effect ahead of those researches which are already final research for the Medical Research Agency, e.g. This is about clinical trials, which is the Agency for Medical Research will support clinical trials to a much greater extent, and we will rather support those studies that may possibly produce products that should then be subjected to clinical trials. So that here there is a line of cooperation, there is no problem with the disorder of the entire cycle of research. They amount to 5% of the fund's value and if we compare this cost to maintenance, for example. agencies such as NCN or NCBR, this is a much lower cost. This is also one of the arguments behind launching such funds, because they work much more flexibly and respond to what is happening in the research area, which does not mean that there is no need for agencies that are more stable in research. We need both. Oh, why is this agency, this fund being set up in the election year? The work on the fund began actually in December 2017 and I must say that we thought that this fund would be set up earlier, but it turned out that this project was so complicated that we could only present it to the High House today. So it's rather a slight delay than the intended goal. Mrs Borowiak asked what the next steps would be. This is a very interesting and good question, because actually the purpose of the fund is not to set up the fund itself. The aim of the fund is to support the potential of Polish scientists, who in the area of medical research could start work today, whose effects will most likely be known in 5 or 10 years. So we're not talking about research that's going to end, but we're talking about starting it or pointing out research fields that are going to be the future of research. This is an iron fund, 500 million is an iron fund for those dozens of teams that will be set up so that they can carry out research in the long term. And that's a little bit of an answer. And your question is why we're calling another institution. Because the grant system, which operates in the NCN and the NCBR, is based on short-term grants, two - three-year-olds. This is actually an international standard - an international standard for this type of institution. But we were inspired by the Flemish example. I think Mrs Schmidt asked if we were making benchmarking or if we were learning from those who did it best. Yeah, we're studying. The WIP is based on the inspiration of the Flemish model, where, unlike the typical agencies that finance research, a small group of research teams from different institutions and disciplines were financed, but in long-term funding. Five to ten years makes it possible for scientists to risk their careers, to risk starting in discipline or in the field of research where no one has been active so far. If this is just a matter of 2 and 3 years, then the risk is simply too big, and we need to, we should explore phenomena where no one has research competence today, because only in this way will we be able to build competition in the future not only scientific but also economic. We proceed with the examination of item 2 of the agenda: First reading of the government draft law amending the Act on the exchange of tax information with other countries and some other laws (print No 3208). I ask the Undersecretary of State at the Ministry of Finance of Mr Filip Świtała to present the justification for the bill.
ParlaMint-PL_2019-03-13-sejm-78-1.u58	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! The amendment to the Act concerns a number of issues. It modifies the rules and procedures for the exchange of tax information, regulates in a slightly different way the obligations of financial institutions to automatically exchange information and control the performance of these duties, and also the obligations for the automatic exchange of information from information on entities forming part of a group of entities, i.e. individuals, but companies. In addition, the Act contains minor modifications to the Tax Order concerning the content of requests for individual tax interpretations, including the implementation of the Information Exchange Directives, and provides for the relaxation of certain obligations concerning the reporting of bank accounts by traders. This is because the Ministry of Finance already has such data, as they are transferred within the framework of the STIR system introduced by us in 2018. Changes in the automatic exchange of tax information aim to eliminate doubts about the application of the legislative measures initially adopted. To a large extent, these modifications were necessary in view of the concerns of the European Commission services regarding the details of the implementation of the EU Directives. The failure to introduce legislative changes could therefore result in the Commission initiating proceedings against Poland. We have therefore decided in the dialogue with the Commission services to make the changes that we present in this law. These changes relate primarily to the establishment of a catalogue of participating countries and countries subject to reporting obligations. These are due to the continuous dynamic process of joining countries in the mechanism for exchanging tax information. The point is that in our legislation we have provided for annual publication in the announcement of the catalogue of reporting countries, while many countries, as well as territories that do not have state status, but want to participate in the exchange mechanism, tax havens in quotes, territories that do not have tax paradise status, but from which we would also like to receive information on assets collected in accounts and in banks by Polish residents, proceed to this exchange mechanism within a year. We would like to be able to include them in the exchange mechanism, not to wait until we can include them in the next year's notice, but to include information that they have already provided during that year. This is the case for collecting information from them, but we leave the notice mechanism to the extent that we require our financial institutions to report these data received from countries entering the exchange mechanism. Here remains the mechanism of the notice, which will work in the same way as today, i.e. by March of each subsequent calendar year we announce - that is to say it will be like today - in the form of a notice, which jurisdictions, banks are supposed to show us the data for the previous year. This obligation for banks will always be the deadline of 30 June of the calendar year, while the exchange itself, appropriate, automatic, takes place until 30 September. Thus, the services of the National Tax Administration provide information received from banks by 30 September to all jurisdictions and countries that have entered the exchange mechanism. We are introducing provisions requiring the submission of declarations of tax residence under criminal liability. This is to ensure effective implementation and compliance with due diligence procedures and effective implementation of the standard of automatic exchange of information. In order to achieve some such systemic coherence, we extend this obligation also to testimony, statements made under the Law implementing the exchange with the United States, or FATCA. In addition, for the period from 1 January 2016 to 30 April 2017, we introduce additional verification of bank account holders. The point is that the persons holding accounts during this period whose tax residence the financial institutions may have certain doubts, the institutions have questions about what their tax residence is and that these persons should provide this information. Now criminal liability does not apply to the obligation for individuals with accounts to provide information. On the other hand, if someone gives it to them, they should provide the real information. And in this case, this rigor will apply. Again, this is an obligation which stems from the reservations of the European Commission services to correct the implementation of the Directive. We believe that this is a better solution, because an alternative solution in this case would be the need to close bank accounts, and this is definitely what the Ministry of Finance wants to avoid. The Act also provides for the obligation to re-identify account holders in cases where the so-called "ongoing account holders' are to be re-identified. an electronic search has not been clearly established that the person has a tax residence in a country. So it's all about the so-called. electronic search of persons who have bank accounts, apply explicitly the tax residence criterion. Therefore, some of the changes we are making here require changes in the IT systems of financial institutions. Therefore, in the Act we want to ensure appropriate transition periods. The second important group of changes, this is a group of changes concerning the so-called. The country-by-country reporting obligation. This is an obligation that affects very large groups of connected economic operators, so-called. international groups which, in addition, in principle exceed the turnover threshold of EUR 750 million per year. So, in principle, these are very large entities. And we make a few recommendations, this time addressed to us by the OECD, by changing the rules for currency conversion. The amendment consists of reference to the method of calculating the threshold amount adopted in the State of the parent undertaking in respect of the entity group concerned in order to ensure the uniformity of the exchange of that information concerning transactions between related parties. In addition, there are a number of minor changes, such as: the legal basis for the agents to sign information about a group of entities and notifications, the delay in submitting information on a group of entities until the end of the third month following the end of the financial year of the group of entities - it is important not to make it so that this obligation may be different, because the different entities in the group are of different years, let's say, financial - requiring that the additional information or explanations contained in the group of entities should also be provided in English. In practice, this is already the case because, as a rule, this information is prepared at group headquarters level and because it is exchanged, it is accepted in practice that it is drawn up in English. In addition, we clarify the issue of administrative sanctions for failure to provide information about the group, but we also extend these sanctions to cases where incomplete documents or incorrect data are submitted. The other two amendments that we make to this law are amendments to requests for interpretation of tax law. We would like to require that the State or territory of the place of residence or residence, as appropriate, be indicated, i.e. data identifying a legal person or an organisational unit or a natural person established or resident in a State or territory in which cross-border effects may occur or have occurred. However, this obligation will not include requests for an individual interpretation concerning an individual case only and requests for an individual interpretation on excise duty and on goods and services tax. The addition of these regulations takes into account the objective of the Directive on the exchange of information on the so-called '. tax interpretations. The situation is that we in Poland spend about 30,000. individual interpretations per year, while other countries - and I do not want to mention here, but they are those countries which had significant doubts about, say, the correctness of their procedures in this regard - issue interpretations that provide some tax advantages. Most often this happens in such a way that these tax advantages are provided at the expense of other jurisdictions and Poland is often a injured party here. We therefore very much want to properly implement the exchange mechanisms in this area and that we, even though it is organisationally difficult for us, meet all the requirements so that we also have the opportunity to receive the same information from other Member States. It is precisely those countries where, as we suspect, certain irregularities may be involved. This change is in line with OECD recommendations based on BEPS and is intended to enable an efficient exchange of tax interpretations with cross-border implications, and we are therefore also very interested in this. The last change is a minor modification of the Tax Order, which abolishs the obligation for banks to inform about bank accounts established for business purposes. This information has so far been provided by banks collectively on time, at such intervals up to 15 days. Since in July 2018 we introduced, as I mentioned, the STIR mechanism, which provided us with much further information, we want to eliminate the double obligation, as is currently the reporting of these data concerning the bank accounts of companies. We give up this obligation that has existed so far. It is quite anachronistic because this information about numbers and companies was not transmitted in electronic form. Today, we have something much better in terms of communicating this information. We give up unnecessary duty. It's all on my side. Thank you very much and I ask the High House to pass this bill in the future. Thank you. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2019-03-13-sejm-78-1.u59	Thank you. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear five minutes' statements on behalf of clubs and circles. I'm opening a discussion. The first vote will be taken by Mrs Maria Zuba, Parliamentary Club Law and Justice.
ParlaMint-PL_2019-03-13-sejm-78-1.u60	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! I have the honour to present to the High House the position of Law and Justice on the draft law amending the Act on the exchange of tax information with other countries and certain other laws. First of all, I would like to stress that Law and Justice will support the entry of these provisions into the legal order of Poland. We're requesting further investigation of this bill. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! Minister! In this draft law, the applicant proposes to introduce changes to the Polish legal order in several areas, among others. improving the rules and procedures for the exchange of tax information, clarifying the obligations of financial institutions with regard to the automatic exchange and control of tax information and sealing the obligations for the automatic exchange of tax information on entities forming part of a group of entities. The provisions in force so far need to be clarified due to the numerous interpretation needs of their importance. In the field of exchange of tax information, the government proposes inter alia. Extension of allowances, which will increase the operational efficiency of handling this issue. According to the rules in force so far, in order for information to be exchanged, the Head of the National Tax Administration shall conclude an agreement with the relevant authority of the Member State. The agreement shall allow authorised representatives of the competent authority of the Member State to be present at the premises of the tax authorities, including in the course of tax proceedings and control activities. Proposed by the applicant in Article. Article 19 (1) (a) and (b) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 1 The amendment allows representatives of the competent authority of the Member State to participate in tax information procedures. In addition, the proposal proposes that representatives should have the right to ask questions to the witness and the party within the framework of the evidence carried out, access to documents, as well as the right to review and maintain them. The government proposes that evidence may be carried out in the course of the proceedings in order to obtain tax information. Following the entry into force of this provision, the Polish tax authority will be required to conduct proceedings within the scope set out in the request of the Member State authority. For the sake of clarity of the proceedings, when the competent authority of the Member State makes an application initiating an administrative case which may relate to a request for written information from the interview of the designated taxable person as a witness, the drafter in Article. 23 indicate the provisions of the Tax Order according to which provisions similar to those adopted in case of application should be applied in this process. As regards the presence or participation in the case of VAT, the same rules as for indirect and direct taxes have been adopted in order to harmonise measures. With regard to the automatic exchange of tax information, the government introduces a clarification of several definitions adopted in the dictionary in order to avoid the need to interpret their meaning. In addition, the proposal introduces in its provisions the principle of providing information which is limited to the scope set out in the proposal by Member States to increase operational efficiency at the level of action. [[♪ The bell ♪]] Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! As I said at the outset, the Law and Justice Club is in favour of further legal proceedings and will support the proposed amendments. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2019-03-13-sejm-78-1.u61	Thank you. I would like to ask the floor of Mrs Zofia Czernow, PO-KO.
ParlaMint-PL_2019-03-13-sejm-78-1.u62	Mrs. Marshal! High Seym! Minister! I have the honour to present the position of the Civic Platform Parliamentary Club - Civic Coalition on the government draft law amending the Act on the exchange of tax information with other countries and certain other laws, print No 3208. The proposed law modifies the rules and procedures for the exchange of tax information, the obligations of financial institutions in the field of automatic exchange of tax information and the control of their implementation. The aim of the project is to eliminate doubts about the application of the legislative measures initially adopted, including, in the context of the objectives of the 2014 and 2016 Council Directives, the guidelines for a multilateral agreement on the automatic exchange of tax information and the Anti-Money Laundering and Terrorist Financing Act. I'll focus on the most important things. The most reservations from financial institutions are raised by Article. 6 of the project, which requires additional verification of the financial accounts opened between 1 January 2016 and 30 April 2017. According to the project, reporting financial institutions will be required to apply to their clients with a request, I stress: with a request for a statement of the tax residence present at the date of the opening of the account. During a very short consultation of the project - only 7 days - most financial institutions raised the issue of high risk of banks being duplicated frequently already performed reporting obligations. The most controversial is the obligation to request historical statements to the client even when the customer has already made such statements. This may result in a significant increase in the number of complaints and may affect the risk of a loss of confidence in financial institutions. In many cases, customers may not respond at all to such a request. Financial institutions shall request that the obligation to re-submit statements be limited only to customers who have not previously submitted them. Insurance companies estimate that there are over 200,000 of them in this group. customers, and this applies not only to insurance companies, but also, and perhaps above all, to banks. The draft provides that, in the absence of a declaration, the financial institution will continue to make contact with the account holder in writing, by telephone, in person or by e-mail. It's hard to get a more imprecise record. We will be thinking, I think, on the resolution of this disputed issue where the financial institutions are very right in the course of further legislative work. Moreover, the Tax Order, which I would like to stress in particular, amends the provisions concerning the extension from 1 day to 2 months of the period during which banks are required to provide information on the accounts of fixed-term deposits. I agree with this provision, but I would like to highlight one point. Couldn't it have been predicted before? We are only now amending the Act, although it was known earlier that it was not possible to provide this information within 1 day. The impact of the draft law is mostly an unpredictable increase in regulatory burdens, an increase in the number of documents and, in particular, the number of procedures. For financial institutions, these are additional costs of changing electronic systems and increasing employment. It should be stressed that most of the changes contained in the project result from excessive haste in lawmaking and from errors and interpretation ambiguities. The directives have not changed. ParlaMint-PL_2019-03-13-sejm-78-1.u65	Thank you. Please take the floor of Mrs Genowefa Tokarska, the Polish People's Party - the Union of European Democrats.
ParlaMint-PL_2019-03-13-sejm-78-1.u66	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! On behalf of the Club of the Polish People's Party - Union of European Democrats, I will present my position on the government's draft law amending the Act on the exchange of tax information with other countries and some other laws, print no. 3208. The draft law modifies the rules and procedures for the exchange of tax information, the obligations of financial institutions for the automatic exchange of tax information and for the control of their implementation, as well as the obligations for the automatic exchange of information on entities forming part of a group of entities. The aim of the proposed law is to eliminate doubts about the application of the solutions already adopted. The regulation is a complement to existing obligations, but, as the government emphasises, this is not the introduction of completely new legislative solutions. The project concerns the banking sector, investment fund associations, insurance companies, brokerage houses and SKOKs. The amendment takes into account the legislative solutions originally adopted, which are, inter alia, based on this. Council Directives 2014/107 and 2016/881, which amended Directive 2011/16 as regards mandatory automatic exchange of information in the field of taxation. The amendments are also based on the guidelines related to the multilateral agreement on automatic exchange of financial information, the guidelines of the Organisation for Economic Cooperation and Development, as well as the Anti-Money Laundering and Terrorist Financing Act. The draft provides for consistency and complementing the statutory definitions and requires additional verification of financial accounts opened between 1 January 2016 and 30 April 2017 as regards compliance with the international standard for automatic exchange of information. The project also introduces regulations on trusts and entities belonging to a group of entities by establishing inter alia. the definition of the group of entities and the calculation of the threshold amount. It also introduces amendments to the Act on the Implementation of the Agreement between the Government of the Republic of Poland and the Government of the United States of America on improving the fulfilment of international tax obligations, which are envisaged inter alia. the extension of the exemption from reporting on the US NIP and the introduction of a declaration of tax residence under criminal liability. In addition to the basic amendment of the Act of 9 March 2017 on the exchange of tax information with other countries, the draft also amends three basic documents: the 1997 Tax Order, as regards the rules for the interpretation of tax law, the Act of 10 September 1999. The Tax Criminal Code, where the introduction of a type of privileged offence is envisaged in the case of actions contrary to the provisions of the Tax Information Exchange Act with other countries, and the Act of 5 July 2018. - i.e. fresh, a year has not passed - on the amendment of the Tax Order and some other laws, where it is proposed to extend from 1 day to 2 months the period during which the banks will be required to provide information on the accounts of the fixed-term investment of the eligible entity. In conclusion, I confirm the assessment of my previous speakers that this project is the result of lawmaking in an unbiased way, without proper consultation, discussion and at a rate that is unacceptable, which I have often pointed out in my previous speeches, but of course we must work on the project, so we are in favour of referring it to the committee. Thank you. [[Applause]]

So, as a general rule, these effects occur in other countries, hence the need to exchange information in this regard. Maria Zuba asked for a broader history of this directive and asked if it was true that what we are introducing is merely the introduction of the directive. This is merely the introduction of the provisions of the Directives. Why are we doing this? In international practice, ex post evaluation of implementation is carried out. This is not just a Polish problem. As part of the OECD, there is a peer review process, i.e. countries which are parties to the Convention, which assess each other's implementation in other countries. And we, too, have recommendations resulting from this process, among others. This is where we implement it. The European Commission does the same. It looks at how it is implemented in different countries, at the full picture of how different countries understand what is written in the directive, and makes recommendations to the Member States on how this needs to be harmonised and improved. As a member of the European Union, we will not avoid this. It's not like the directive... If we could, if you ever allow it, rewrite a directive that contains technical regulations, and simply put it to the vote, that's okay, then we'll always be okay, but that's not the process of implementing the directive, we have to introduce it into the Polish legal order. The Commission will always assess the effects of such implementation at European Union level and we will not avoid the possibility of such situations in the future. It's just that, it's complicated matter. In 2015, there was time to implement it in its entirety so that it could start in 2016, because that was the original deadline from which it should apply. All the perturbations that have emerged now are due to the fact that it did not come into force in 2016. If we had kept this deadline, we would have had a statement on 1 January 2016 and everything would be fine. Meanwhile, in 2015 there were delays in the implementation of this directive, we did not implement it in time and this delay resulted in something that the Directive does not foresee, i.e. a transitional period between 1 January 2016 and 30 April 2017, which we must specially treat. At the level of negotiations with the Commission services, it was agreed how we should do this. So that maybe sometimes we implement something quickly, but in this case we do so to correct errors that have been made much, much earlier, when there was a lot of time to implement this directive, but unfortunately it was not enough. Mr Paweł Grabowski's question was about changing the content of the basis. ParlaMint-PL_2019-03-13-sejm-78-1.u90	Again, I apologize for the confusion. On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, Law and Justice, I present an opinion on the report of the Committee on Public Finance in print No 3248 on the Government's draft law amending the Financial Market Surveillance Act and certain other laws contained in print No 3205. Regulation of the European Parliament and of the Council of 12 December 2017 on the establishment of a general legal framework for securitisation is a prerequisite for the amendment of that law. Regulation No 2017/2402 is directly applicable in the countries of the European Union as from 1 January 2019. However, in order to make the regulation more effective, it is necessary to amend the provisions of Polish law, in particular the Law of 21 July 2006 on supervision of the financial market, but also several other laws: banking law, the Law of Entrepreneurs, investment funds or the Ombudsman. The proposed amendment extends the supervisory powers of the Financial Supervision Commission to supervision of securitisation processes and confers control powers on the supervisory authority - KNF - of securitisation participants in respect of compliance with legal standards. Accordingly, for the purposes of the audit, the Financial Supervision Authority shall be granted the right to request explanations from the entities audited and, to the extent necessary, to inspect financial documents such as accounting, commercial, regulations, instructions, copies, copies of documents and extracts thereof, including those documents which are confidential and protected by law. The Commission for Financial Supervision will also have the right to conduct investigations under this Law. In order to make supervision effective, KNF will be empowered, in accordance with the project, to take corrective measures and impose administrative sanctions by decision for infringement of the Regulation. The sanctions envisaged are primarily the suspension in the activities of the member of the board responsible for the infringement, the prohibition of a member of the management board or management functions in the entities involved in the securitisation, the fines up to the amount specified in the Act. Thus, the fines included in the Act are amounts not exceeding PLN 20 869 500 - from both legal persons and natural persons - or twice the amount of benefits obtained or losses avoided as a result of the infringement when it is possible to determine them. The monetary penalty is fixed in the Law at the said level, but an alternative penalty may be applied: 10% of the net revenue from the sale of goods and services and the financial operations for the last financial year resulting from the approved report. The rules that apply to insurance undertakings are also regulated. In addition to the sanctions discussed, they should also make public the information on the sanctions covered by the entities on the website of the Financial Supervision Commission. The proposed amendments, on the one hand, are forced by a regulation of the European Parliament, but on the other hand, they also give an opportunity to ensure the proper functioning of the financial markets, transparency and security of this funding channel, which is securitisation of claims as a source of financing for companies and lending to banks. Parliamentary Club Law and Justice supports the bill and proposes two amendments to the bill. One is specific, the other is mixed, precise and substantive. Here you go, these are corrections.

ParlaMint-PL_2019-03-13-sejm-78-1.u95	Thank you. Please take the floor of Mrs Genowefa Tokarska, the Polish People's Party - the Union of European Democrats.
ParlaMint-PL_2019-03-13-sejm-78-1.u96	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! On behalf of the Club of the Polish People's Party - the Union of European Democrats, I have the honour to present my position on the committee's report on the draft law amending the Law on Financial Market Surveillance and some other laws, in print no. 3205 and 3248. The draft law aims at implementing, implementing Regulation 2017/2402 of the European Parliament and of the Council of 12 December 2017 establishing a general framework for securitisation and establishing a specific framework for simple, transparent and standard securitisation and amending other Directives of the European Union and regulations on the subject. The draft defines the concept of securitisation as a transaction or a programme whereby the credit risk associated with the exposure or pool of exposures is broken down into tranches. The Regulation entered into force on 17 January 2018 and its provisions are already directly applicable as from 1 January 2019 in all Member States. Under our Polish conditions, the draft law identifies the Financial Supervision Commission as the authority competent to supervise compliance with the rules and performance of the obligations set out in the Union Regulation on securitisation. In addition, the draft provides the Financial Supervision Commission with the power to apply administrative sanctions and remedies, as well as to publish information on the website of the Financial Supervision Authority on the imposition of such sanctions and on access to confidential information. The entities involved in the securitisation are banks, securitisation funds, leasing and insurance companies and individual and institutional investors. Those entities should comply with due diligence, transparency, risk retention and application of credit criteria and requirements for the sale of securitisation positions to clients. The securitisation of claims in the long term creates additional investment opportunities for companies and provides an alternative source of capital. The first reading of the project, as my previous speakers said, took place at the meeting of the Committee on Public Finance. During the meeting, the committee adopted three amendments aimed at improving communication between public oversight authorities and introducing in the legal order solutions compatible with European Union law. My club, the Members' Club of the Polish People's Party - the Union of European Democrats is in favour of further legislative work on this project. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2019-03-13-sejm-78-1.u110	Dear Marshal! Dear Members, I have the honour to present a draft law on the regulation of certain matters in connection with the appearance of the United Kingdom of Great Britain and Northern Ireland from the European Union and the European Atomic Energy Community without the conclusion of the agreement referred to in Article. The Commission shall adopt implementing acts laying down detailed rules for the implementation of this Regulation in accordance with Regulation (EU) No 182/2011 of the European Parliament and of the Council laying down detailed rules for the implementation of this Regulation. 2 of the Treaty on European Union, print No 3256. The regulations contained in the draft go against the declaration by the European Union to ensure the protection of the rights of citizens of the United Kingdom in EU countries, which also stresses that citizens of the European Union in the United Kingdom should also not suffer negative consequences in the absence of an agreement between the European Union and the United Kingdom for the withdrawal of the United Kingdom of Great Britain and Northern Ireland from the European Union and the European Atomic Energy Community. In the absence of an entry into force scenario on 30 March 2019, the entire original and secondary European Union law will cease to apply to the United Kingdom on the date of its withdrawal from the European Union and the European Atomic Energy Community. This country will become a third country and therefore the citizens of the United Kingdom and members of their families residing in other Member States of the European Union, including Poland, will lose their residence rights resulting from European Union citizenship and further rights related to that status held by that date, that date and become non-regulated third-country nationals. In accordance with the Communication from the European Commission of 13 November 2018 on the preparations for the non-contractual withdrawal of the United Kingdom from the European Union to the European Parliament, the European Council, the Council of the European Central Bank, the European Economic and Social Committee, the Committee of the Regions and the European Investment Bank on the In preparation for the United Kingdom's withdrawal from the European Union on 30 March 2019, the contingency plan during negotiations with the United Kingdom, the European Union has put citizens first from the outset. In line with the conclusions of the European Council of 29 April 2017, citizens' issues form the first part of the draft agreement on substantive law. The European Union has always felt that citizens should not suffer the negative consequences of leaving, so-called. Brexite. This requires Member States to take a favourable approach to the rights of citizens of the United Kingdom who already live in their territory. In this context, the Commission welcomes the assurance of the Prime Minister of the United Kingdom Government that even if there is no agreement, the rights of citizens of the European Union in the United Kingdom will be protected. The proposed bill sets out the principles, first, of residence in Poland of citizens of the United Kingdom and members of their families who will benefit from the Treaty freedom of movement. Secondly, the granting and payment of social security benefits taking into account periods of work and residence in the United Kingdom. Thirdly, doing business in Poland by foreign companies from the United Kingdom providing employment services in our country. Fourthly, the posting of an employee to Poland from the United Kingdom. Fifthly, the protection of employees' claims in the event of insolvency of a foreign employer from the United Kingdom. Sixth, participation in the economic trade of British foreign persons, accession of UK nationals and members of their families to universal health insurance until they are granted a temporary residence permit or permanent residence permit, recognition of documents issued in the United Kingdom confirming the ownership of driving powers and the continuation of judicial executions carried out on the basis of judgments of the courts of the United Kingdom. One of the most important issues covered by the bill concerns residence issues. According to the new regulations, British residents and members of their families living in Poland from the moment of their departure from the European Union, assuming that this will happen on 30 March 2019, will have 21 months to submit their application in order to confirm their residence status in Poland by 31 December. The Act sets the date of submission of this application and the starting date of that period, taking into account the date of departure of the United Kingdom from the EU, which will be specified in the Polish Monitor. So that it is not a strict date for today referred to as March 31st. The right to apply for a permanent or temporary residence permit will be granted to all British people who had a right of residence or a right of permanent residence in our country prior to the so-called residence permit. Brexitem and their family members will also have the right to do so. The Act also provides that, after 31 December 2020, these proposals will no longer be dealt with. Authorisations on the basis of applications submitted will be exempt from fees. Persons who will have permanent residence in Poland on 29 March 2019 during a transitional period, i.e. by 31 December 2020, they will be able to apply for a permanent residence permit. Other persons will be able to apply for a temporary residence permit for the period of 5 years. After this period, i.e. after 5 years of legal stay in Poland, also after so-called. Brexita British and members of their families will be able to obtain a permanent residence permit in the Polish Republic under general conditions. According to the submitted project, the British and members of their families whose stay in Poland will be recognised as legal, from 30 March 2019 to 31 December 2020 and during the temporary residence or permanent residence proceedings, will have the right to work and to continue their business activity already undertaken. In conclusion, I would like to emphasise once again that the date specified in the draft will refer to the actual date of Britain's departure from the European Union, which will, as I mentioned, be initially specified in the Polish Monitor. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2019-03-13-sejm-78-1.u142	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! To answer the repeated questions of Members of Parliament in many ways, I would like to start by pointing out that on 6 December 2018, the UK Government published a document called "Citizens.' Rights - EU Citizen in the UK and UK Nationals in the EU - Policy Paper." It is a document in which the British Government presents the assumptions of British legislative solutions aimed at protecting citizens' rights in the case of brexit without agreement. With regard to the provisions of this document, we can say that the UK guarantees regarding the protection of the citizens of the European Union in the event of a non-contractual brexite are largely based on the solutions adopted in the draft exit agreement from the European Union. First of all, I would like to point out that the solutions proposed in the draft law are based on the principle of reciprocity. We have the guarantee made by Prime Minister May during her visit to our country, repeatedly confirmed by the British Government that the principle of reciprocity will apply to Polish citizens living in the UK. First of all, I would like to point out that the main differences compared to the Brexit carried out in reaching an agreement on the Brexit Agreement would concern, first, the lack of jurisdiction of the Court of Justice of the European Union. This applies to possible further effects on justice. Secondly, the date by which the right to remain in the United Kingdom will be determined from 31 December 2020 to 29 March 2019 will be postponed. That's the difference. The third point is, of course, the restrictions on bringing family members to the UK. However, it was withdrawn. The UK proposal provides that, in the absence of a contract, European Union citizens who have resided in the UK before a possible Brexit will be able to stay on its territory. This applies to our citizens who will have the right to apply for a free stay in the UK by 31 December 2020. If they do not, after this date their stay will be subject to new migration rules. Of course, in case their stay until then is still illegal. Such a proposal will then be dealt with in a simplified procedure similar to that envisaged, as I mentioned, in the draft withdrawal agreement. In answer to Mr Wójcik's question on this occasion, I would like to add that the right of residence and the right to permanent residence are not dependent on the possession of documents relevant to the citizens of the European Union. According to the project, possession of documents is intended to simplify the procedure. I would also like to mention issues relating to people residing in the UK legally for at least five years. These persons will have the opportunity to obtain the status of a resident providing them with protection of their rights for the rest of their lives. This status is also based on the solutions contained in the contract. It can only be lost after 5 years of absence in the UK. Persons who stay there for less than 5 years until their residence application has been lodged will be granted the status of a temporary resident and, after this period, after 5 years of legal residence, may apply for the status of a resident. Another setup. Within 3 years of the date of Brexit, EU citizens who are in the Islands will be able to join their family members if the family relationship existed on this contractual date of Brexit and the date of arrival in the UK. In the case of marriages concluded after the brexite, such spouses will be able to come to the UK and will reside there until 31 December 2020. This is also, as I say, the regulation proposed in our bill. The issue of professional qualifications and recognition. They will be protected in the same way as in the case of a contract brexite. With regard to the protection of qualifications already recognised and completed under the current rules of proceedings still pending on the date of brexitu, this will not change after the date of brexitu. Another issue, concerning other regulations, is access to the labour market, doing business - this was already mentioned in the project discussion - access to public services, health care, education. Poles will maintain their rights at this level, which is now, that is, the level envisaged, once again, in the draft exit agreement. I would like to point out that until 31 December 2020 entry into the United Kingdom will be possible on the basis of an identity card, as is currently the case. As of 1 January 2021, a new migration system is to apply and new rules for entry will be laid down. I would like to add, to discuss, that Poles who will come to the UK between 30 March 2019 and 31 December 2020 will have to apply for a three-year temporary residence permit. After this period, of course, as I mentioned, new migration rules will apply. With regard to Mr Lipc's question as to whether the bill is intended to be amended, I would like to say that the proposal does not provide for an amendment. Of course, if this is required by the situation after the UK exits, depending on the regulations that will apply, we will adapt our rules to these regulations. In conclusion, I will give the number of citizens of Great Britain who live in the territory of the Republic of Poland today. That's less than 6,000. Exactly as far as residence permits are concerned, this is slightly over 5,7 thousand.; I am talking about residence permits for temporary stay and permanent residence. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2019-03-13-sejm-78-1.u144	Mrs. Marshal! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, I am pleased to once again present to you the most important issues of Polish policy during the next Presidency of the European Union, this time the Austrian Presidency. Referring to the previous debate, I can stress that the very important event in this Presidency in the context of brexitu was, of course, the adoption by the European Council of an exit agreement which regulates all the important aspects of Britain's exit from the European Union, including civil issues, and is certainly the best form of protection of the rights of Polish citizens in the UK and the rights of British citizens in Poland. However, in view of the increasing risk of the collapse of this agreement due to the ratification crisis in London, we must be prepared to take emergency action. The key element of the emergency action from a Polish point of view is, of course, the adoption of this law, which is primarily intended to recreate a very wide range of protection of rights, which is appropriate for EU regulations, on a reciprocal basis, with the British side. I think it is a sincere intention, repeatedly confirmed by Prime Minister Morawiecki and Prime Minister May, so that the turbulence associated with the Brexit process does not cause any harm to citizens' rights. Citizens should not be held hostage by this political process and we are doing everything we can to prevent this from happening. Of course, the issue of the rights of Poles living in the UK has been crucial for us from the very beginning, but this is not the only issue that is important in this process. As regards the work of the Presidency, in addition to the adoption of this agreement, security issues deserve special attention. The Presidency has shown that this is a key priority for Austria by organising an informal summit in Salzburg on security issues, including in the context of migration. Legally, we had a very reasonable increase in the work on the exchange of information in EURODOCSIS and VIS. This interoperability of systems is crucial in terms of effective control of passenger traffic at the external borders of the European Union, both for the citizens of the European Union and for those who enjoy visa-free travel with the Member States of the European Union, and of course for those who must have visas. We believe that the credibility of border controls at the external borders of the European Union is the key to maintaining the Schengen system, the key to maintaining full freedom of travel within the European Union, and that is why we strongly supported the completion of work in these areas. The Presidency drew attention to improving cybersecurity, which is why ENISA has been strengthened, which is responsible for network security in the Union. There has also been a preliminary agreement on the ECRIS system, which is responsible for full ease, full availability of criminal records data. As a result of the shocking chemical attack in the UK in Salisbury, Austria, as Presidency, raised the issue of building greater resilience to chemical, biological, radiological and nuclear attacks at European Union level. This was discussed at the European Council level. The need for sanctions for the use of chemical weapons in the European Union has also been established. We have agreed on the most difficult aspect of this agenda, namely the reform of the European asylum system. We have agreed to strengthen the mandate of the European Border and Coast Guard, in particular on cooperation with third countries and on return policy. The Warsaw-based agency, formerly known as Frontex, will have more opportunities to cooperate with third countries in order to better respond to the problem of uncontrolled migratory pressure at our external borders. There has been a conceptual and political breakthrough, I stress, not yet, in the reform of the asylum system. In line with Polish expectations, after more than three years of efforts, often lonely, over time in an increasing number of countries, a huge breakthrough has been achieved in terms of rejecting any form of transnational management of migration flows or asylum. In this respect, we appreciate the great dedication, the sincere work of the Austrian Presidency. During this period we had the opportunity to host a special Chancellor's envoy in Warsaw on this matter. The Presidency drew the right conclusions from the groundbreaking conclusions negotiated by Prime Minister Morawiecki in June by presenting a conceptual document which was intended to pave the way for the full implementation of the decisions we expected. Unfortunately, we are still waiting for the legislative confirmation of these actions, which is why this pressure must be maintained - it must be maintained at national level, it must be maintained at European Parliament level, it must be maintained against the European Commission. The second important pillar of the Presidency's work was economic issues, which were broadly understood by economic issues. The Presidency has tried to better prepare the process of negotiating the multiannual financial framework. This process isn't advanced today, the reasons aren't random. We are dealing with historically the greatest diversity in Member States' positions on the multiannual financial framework, which makes the forthcoming negotiations of the multiannual financial framework the most historically difficult, with all certainty unprecedented. Poland continues to stress that financing new tasks, in particular those relating to defence and migration, cannot be done in a simple way at the expense of traditional policies. We stress the importance of agricultural policy not only for the internal market, in the face of growing trade pressures from the outside, also from jurisdictions which do not apply very many high requirements which we are putting on the market for Europe, as well as for Polish farmers. In the context of cohesion policy, we present first and foremost Polish experiences on this issue. Cohesion policy was an important factor in social and economic convergence, which in turn eliminates, or at least reduces, unwanted wage competition or tax competition, which is criticised by some countries involved in the budgetary debate. We welcome the adoption of ambitious provisions on the internal market strategy. This is the key dimension of Polish European policy. Unfortunately, we note that these very strong declarations do not follow the facts. There is a continuing deadlock in legislation in the area of the freight package, in the scope of the notification directive or in the eCard directive. This is not groundbreaking, but important legislation that should ensure better openness, greater availability of the services market, which is extremely important for the Polish economy. However, strong political declarations unfortunately do not translate into appropriate legislative solutions. We are still facing persistent protectionist pressure, and it is often done by countries that are willing to wave the EU flag. Poland is one of the most pro-European parts of the coalition of countries which insist that the Community market, the fundamental dimension of the Treaty's cooperation on European integration, should be implemented seriously, not only by political declarations. The same applies to the construction of the Community digital market, where we note that time is detrimental to Europe, because this market will develop in any case in many other competitive business, economic centres and who else can set standards for this market. We demand that the fundamental dimension of the Community digital market be the free movement of data, i.e., the fifth necessary Treaty freedom for development, the future of this market. Without the full, Treaty- guaranteed freedom of data flow, we will have very limited opportunities to benefit from the added value of the European digital market. Without the freedom of data flow, it is difficult to imagine an effective strategy of artificial intelligence. The third dimension of the action, which we believe is on the verge of external and market issues, was the continuing deadlock of the Gas Directive. The directive did not provide any extraordinary expectations. This expectation was very simple and very basic. Our expectation, like that of the European Commission, was that the European Union should enforce its own rules, its own right in relation to every element of energy infrastructure at our borders. Here I must say that we have been disappointed to see the Austrian Presidency's work, which has put its hand in blocking the work on this directive. This year's impasse on legislative work was interrupted only at the beginning of the Romanian Presidency with a general share of Polish and French pressure. The idea was to finally draw completely basic, nothing unusual, consequences from our own arrangements regarding the shape of the energy market and the shape of energy security. Because in this case, we are talking about a case that goes far beyond the logic of the market alone, beyond the logic of maintaining fair competition in the energy market. This issue is of strategic importance in order to ensure the security of the region of investment Nord Stream. With regard to the neighbourhood, Austria has obviously focused on renewing the accession process in relation to the Western Balkans. This enjoys strong support from Warsaw. We believe that enlargement policy should remain an important element, an instrument of impact on our international environment, in particular in relation to the Western Balkans, where we observe strategic competition of forces, including those quite confronting Europe. The absence of Europe in this region does not mean that nothing will happen there. Disappearance means that these countries will make political choices at the expense of our prosperity, at the expense of our security. Events in the Azov Sea were an extremely important reminder of the importance of the neighbourhood policy, particularly in the eastern aspect. Of course, we appreciate that the 10th anniversary of this policy, the 10th anniversary of the partnership is a perfect time to renew it, but not just for anniversary reasons, we would like the Union to take its responsibility into account in the previously established scope for the eastern neighbourhood. The events in the Azov Sea in the Kerczeń Strait have shown that we need an ambitious presence of the European Union in this area, that the external policy of the European Union should be balanced and settled between the East and the South, because from both directions risks to our security may arise, but none of these directions is less important from the point of view of European interests. This is already the seventh Presidency, which I have the pleasure of reporting to the Polish Parliament, so I think it is a good time to try at least to look at this issue from a slightly broader perspective. Because we are usually forced to deal in total with the details of the European process, sometimes this broader, important picture can escape us. I would like to highlight a few fundamental things. Over the last three years, despite the polexit fantasies that I have unfortunately also heard about in this Chamber, Poland has been a participant in the most pro-European coalition of countries in a dimension which is crucial for the future of the European integration process. This is the dimension of the common market, in particular the common market in services, where we have our competitive advantages, as well as the digital market. It is someone else who causes an impasse, a long-term legislative impasse in these areas, and please answer yourself the question of who it is. [[Who?]] It is the Polish government that has set the path to the necessary migration compromise, which excludes relocation on the one hand, but gives the opportunity for the European Union's joint coordinated action at the external borders of the European Union, which, in line with our experience over the last three years for today, is the only effective means of dealing with the migration crisis in Europe. We are very close to a new opening in the European Union's defence policy, based on Polish expectations - we have been saying this for more than a year - to build the European Union's defence policy in full synergy with NATO. NATO is a key reference to the defence policy of the European Union, Europe and the overwhelming majority of the Member States of the European Union. There is no reason to duplicate decision-making processes, strategies, and in particular our very limited security resources. We pay attention to the need to maintain the strategic balance that I spoke about when reporting on the neighbourhood policy. The South is as important as the East. None of the strategic directions should be ignored when building this new openness in defence policy. We draw attention to the fact that the European Union should be open to the pursuit of all countries that want to maintain the defence industry. This case will be over soon, and it will be decided positively. The European Defence Fund will be open to the Polish defence industry. It will be up to us if we want to take part in this venture. We are increasingly present in PESCO's structural cooperation. Poland is actively present in eight projects, of course in particular those projects that coincide with NATO's strategy. I mean, in particular, military mobility. We are finally open - and this is the last, fourth aspect of our pro-European approach to the political processes that are taking place in the European Union - to discuss the new industrial policy and take into account new circumstances, in particular the growing protectionism of China. We do not exclude discussions on the principle of reciprocity regarding access to markets, in particular public procurement, discussions on market protection instruments or discussions on competition policy. After all, we are already part of these new funding mechanisms, in particular innovative projects with much greater market risks, which are important for the European energy transition, particularly in the field of electromobility. [[♪ The bell ♪]] This is a proposal for change, a project for the future of the European Union, which we would like to leave to the new political cycle in the European Union as a good basis for such a reform of the European Union, which will be comfortable and comfortable for all. ParlaMint-PL_2019-03-13-sejm-78-1.u146	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! I am very pleased to be able, on behalf of the Committee on European Union Affairs, for the eighth time as chairman of this committee, to present a draft resolution of the Sejm on the participation of the Republic of Poland in the work of the European Union in July-December 2018 (the Austrian Presidency of the Council of the European Union). Information from the Council of Ministers on Poland's activities during the Austrian Presidency was adopted by the Committee for European Affairs on 9 January 2019 and forwarded to the High House on 10 January. The government's information, as in previous years, was prepared in cooperation with all ministries and central offices, and the Ministry of Foreign Affairs is responsible for its presentation. This is already the thirty-six-month report of the Council of Ministers for the Sejm and Senate of the Republic of Poland from the participation of the Republic of Poland in the work of the Union during the successive Presidency periods. The European Union Affairs Commission considered the information provided in print 3145 at the committee meeting on 13 February. The information on behalf of the Council of Ministers was presented by Minister Konrad Szymański, State Secretary at the Ministry of Foreign Affairs. The Minister presented the course and achievements of the Austrian Presidency through its priorities, but also key issues from Poland's point of view. The Austrian Presidency has focused its efforts on the three main pillars: security and the fight against illegal migration, prosperity and competitiveness through digitisation, stability in the neighbourhood with particular emphasis on the Western Balkans and the Eastern Partnership. As regards the implementation of the priority: security and the fight against irregular migration, the Austrian Presidency has focused on a comprehensive approach to migration, covering the protection of external borders, cooperation with countries of origin and transit and ensuring the effectiveness of return. The informal summit of Heads of State and Government in Salzburg in September 2018 was a tone of action taken by the Presidency in these areas. Key issues raised by EU leaders were the strengthening of police and judicial cooperation, security at the external borders of the European Union and the fight against cybercrime. In this context Poland welcomed the Presidency's accession to the agreement with the European Parliament on an act on cybersecurity, which is intended to contribute to strengthening the European Union Agency for Foreign Affairs and Security Policy. Network and Information Security and facilitate cooperation between Member States in this area. During the Austrian Presidency of the Council of the European Union, work on the reform of the Common European Asylum System was also advanced. Poland appreciates the Presidency's efforts to achieve a balance between the principles of responsibility and solidarity. The proposed concept of mandatory solidarity is an interesting alternative to compulsory redistribution. Further negotiations on this part of the reform are now being continued by the Romanian Presidency. The second priority of the Presidency is prosperity and competitiveness through digitisation. Work continued on the legislative proposals presented in the framework of the implementation of the Internal Market Strategy and the Digital Single Market Strategy. The Presidency's efforts have led to the conclusion of negotiations on the freight package to ensure effective application of the principle of mutual recognition and thereby improve trade in goods in the single market. Unfortunately, work on initiatives such as the e-service card and the draft notification directive, which would significantly reduce barriers for entrepreneurs, are in the impasse. An important element of the work carried out by the Austrian Presidency was the efforts to complete the Digital Single Market. Poland supported efforts to guarantee the free movement of non-personal data, in particular those of the public sector. Work was also continued on proposals for taxation of digital services. During the Austrian Presidency, the negotiations of the multiannual financial framework for 20212027 continued. In the opinion of Poland, the future budget should be more ambitious and reflect the political priorities of the European Union. The financing of existing policies, new priorities and the financial gap created by brexit must be ensured. The negotiations were scheduled for autumn 2019. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Another challenge faced by the Austrian Presidency was security and stability issues in the neighbourhood with emphasis on the Western Balkans and the role of the Eastern Partnership. The objective is to make concrete progress on the enlargement of the European Union to the countries of the region. Thanks to the involvement of the Austrian Presidency, the Council for Foreign Affairs and Security Policy. The foreign commitment to maintain the EU perspective for Bosnia and Herzegovina as a single country has been confirmed. In addition, 16 negotiating chapters with Serbia were also closed. Within the area of cooperation with neighbouring countries of the European Union, Poland has consistently stressed that it is necessary to maintain a balance in the involvement of the European Union towards the southern and eastern neighbours. The Austrian Presidency organised in October 2018 the Eastern Partnership Foreign Ministers Summit, which this year celebrates the 10th anniversary of the inauguration, which should also be an opportunity to intensify the work on the Eastern Neighbourhood Policy. Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! The Commission has consistently and decisively voted against reducing the level of EU financing for the common agricultural policy after 2020.
