ParlaMint-PL_2019-03-13-sejm-78-1.u187	Thank you very much, sir. Thank you. And please, Mr Marcin Święcicki, Civic Platform - Civic Coalition.
ParlaMint-PL_2019-03-13-sejm-78-1.u188	Mrs. Marshal! High Izbo! The Law amending the Law on the License of Restructuring Advisor refers to more than 1100 restructuring advisors who act as syndicates, supervisors or managers in insolvency proceedings. They conduct their activities throughout the country under the supervision of the relevant courts. This law introduces the supervision of the Minister of Justice of the restructuring advisors in addition to judicial supervision. The Minister of Justice under this law will now be able to request all the documentation produced by the restructuring adviser, will have access to the files completed or to the files of pending proceedings, access to any commercial accounts of traders against whom restructuring or insolvency proceedings have ever been conducted. In addition, it will have the possibility to suspend or revoke the licence of the restructuring adviser. It seems that this far-reaching interference of the Minister of Justice in the restructuring and bankruptcy activities exceeds the real possibilities of the Ministry. Somehow I don't really imagine what a great service a resort would have to have in order to run all this, since it's 1,100, over 1,100 of these advisors across the country, and the Ministry's justification for this law has, for example. examine whether the activities undertaken by the advisors are taking place quickly enough: whether there are delays there or whether there is any loss for this reason. How can you study 1,100 advisors throughout the country and all their activities, whether they are delayed or not delayed? This is some unreal thing. Secondly, we come across the issue of constitutional compatibility, because the constitution in 45 Art. It is clear that every citizen has the right to review his case by an independent, impartial and independent court. In the meantime, here, the Minister of Justice is involved in the case, which is given quite large powers. The prosecutor will have the power to request the cancellation of the restructuring adviser. Finally, another thing that raises concern is that the Act states that the Minister will not interfere in these matters, in these activities, in those activities which the restructuring advisors, the syndicate, the insolvency manager has ordered the court to do, but that he will not interfere in these matters, in those activities which have been commissioned by the creditors' board or by the creditors' assembly. However, the entire restructuring or insolvency proceedings aim to maximise the recovery of what creditors lose from the debtor insolvent. And their decisions can also be challenged by the court, but only if they are against the law. If there are discrepancies between creditors and one of them brings the case before the court, then the judge can settle such contradictions, but if they are legal decisions, they are not contested, then the restructuring adviser must execute the decision of creditors, the board of creditors or the assembly of creditors. Here, the bill doesn't say anything about the minister having to comply with it. It must comply with the court's orders, but it no longer needs to comply with decisions taken by creditors who are, after all, the group most interested in the successful outcome of such proceedings. Therefore, there are significant doubts as to why there is such a great extension of the Ministry's powers to another area where, in fact, without sufficient apparatus, the Minister will be able to intervene very far, suspend or withdraw the powers, the licence of restructuring advisers. Isn't this another area where the government of the Ministry of Justice is expanding without a substantive, sufficient justification? There are important constitutional doubts as to whether he has the right to such a thing. The purpose of this law, which serves to justify it, namely speeding up or avoiding errors, is rather not gaining much, but is gaining the authority of the minister, who will be able to use this power in a much wider way than before. Actually, we have a very... We have tabled some amendments to this bill. One of them - even I see that the PiS has also ascribed to this amendment - is saying that it is only twice the warning that it is impossible to obtain a title [[♪ The bell ♪]] a qualified restructuring adviser. Nevertheless, despite these amendments, the club still leaves time to think about whether to vote on this bill. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2019-03-13-sejm-78-1.u190	Thank you very much. Mrs. Marshal! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, Minister! On behalf of the Club of the Polish People's Party - Union of European Democrats, I would like to present our position on the report of the committee on the government bill amending the law on the license of restructuring advisors, bankruptcy law and the law on restructuring, seym prints no. 2089 and 3226. This draft amendment to the Law on the License of the Restructuring Advisor assumes that the activities of the advisors are to be supervised by the Minister of Justice. The aim of the project developers is to strengthen the supervision of the activities of the advisors, as well as to increase the transparency of the system, their appointment and the delegation of matters of importance to the economic security and defence of the State to those with the highest qualifications and extensive experience in the conduct of restructuring and bankruptcy proceedings. Following the adoption of the proposed law, the Minister of Justice will supervise persons licensed as restructuring advisers independently of judicial supervision. Such supervision shall not relate to the assessment of the activities carried out directly on request, with the permission or agreement of the Commissioner or of the Judge. Under the new provisions, the Minister of Justice will be able to suspend or revoke an advisory licence which has persistently or grossly infringed the law in the performance of its activities. However, this will require a prior hearing of the adviser and, if necessary or at the request of the adviser, to conduct a visit or to illustrate his activities. To this end, the Minister of Justice will be able to request access to the source documents and compare their content with the documentation submitted by the restructuring adviser to the file on restructuring and bankruptcy proceedings. The refusal to provide documentation will be punishable by a fine. The new rules will introduce general criteria to be followed by a court, setting up a restructuring adviser to act, e.g. a syndicate in specific court proceedings. In such a case, the court must take into account the number of cases that the advisor is currently pursuing. In addition, the court should take into account the experience and additional qualifications of the potential candidate to perform the function in question. Information on the number of cases conducted by advisers will be available on the Minister of Justice's website. It was assumed that as far as the groups of licensed advisers are concerned, qualified restructuring advisers will be selected, who, apart from the licence, will have extensive professional experience to deal with complex restructuring and bankruptcy procedures. The acquisition of the title of qualified advisor will require documentation of bankruptcy proceedings and arrangement proceedings carried out. The title of qualified restructuring adviser will be awarded to the Minister of Justice on the basis of the criteria set out in the Act. High Izbo! The current licensing system allows for the admission to the profession of advisors of persons who are well aware of the applicable insolvency and restructuring legislation, but does not guarantee the smooth conduct of proceedings concerning a large business complex entity, for example because of the lack of a requirement to demonstrate targeted experience in obtaining licences. In addition, the existing arrangements do not actually contain criteria for the designation of restructuring advisers by the courts, except where the relevant group of creditors has at least 30% of their activity. the creditors' board shall take a decision or a resolution accordingly. This may have particularly negative consequences in the event of bankruptcy or the need for restructuring of an entrepreneur of particular importance for the security of the state or citizens, the national economy and the labour market. So there is no doubt that there is a need for change in this area, but it is born, High Izbo, the question of whether the right path for the next professional groups is to centralise their control, subjecting them to influence at this moment - I think it is possible to say - the superministership, which is increasingly becoming the Ministry of Justice. The Polish People's Party - Union of European Democrats Members' Club makes the support of this project conditional on further work and amendments [[♪ The bell ♪]] Reported to him. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2019-03-13-sejm-78-1.u210	Mrs. Marshal! Minister! Ladies and gentlemen, ladies and gentlemen, High Izbo! On behalf of the Committee on Infrastructure, I would like to present a report on the Government's draft law amending the Law on Road Traffic and the Penal Code in print No 2878. It is, generally speaking, a law on imposing penalties for shifting counters in vehicles, in cars. The phenomenon of unauthorised interference in the indication of the vehicle's mileage counter includes motor vehicles and is aimed at obtaining an unauthorised advantage by the seller of the vehicle. This was already presented during the first reading of the government presentation. It is worth recalling some key information accompanying this comprehensive regulation prepared by the Ministry of Justice. This law, as I mentioned, amends the Road Traffic Law and the Penal Code. Statistics show that the number of cars with this so-called. The curved meter is about 240 thousand, or more than 1.2%. all cars in Poland. Some data indicate that there are many more. In view of the elimination of this undoubtedly pathological phenomenon, regulation is proposed which is intended to effectively prevent unauthorised interference with the mileage. Here I would also like to recall the Directive of the European Parliament and of the Council of 3 April 2014 on periodic roadworthiness tests for motor vehicles and trailers. This regulation is a European context. Compared to the existing footnotes of the Road Traffic Law and the Penal Code, the draft provides for transitional provisions for implementing acts based on this regulation, and the proposed amendment is primarily intended to guarantee the certainty of trade and prevent unfair practices of at least some persons selling motor vehicles. The aim of the amendments is to eliminate the practice of offering services for the under-seeking of road signs in a mechanical vehicle. As I said, these changes are being implemented into the Road Traffic Law. Some of the proposed amendments create new administrative and legal obligations, which are addressed not only to motor vehicle owners, but also to public authorities and services whose tasks will be extended to prevent and prevent manipulations in road signs. In this part, the project introduces a new sanctioning form in addition to the current state, which allows for the replacement of the roadmeter only if the roadmeter does not measure the course of the vehicle in a situation where, due to its intended purpose, it should measure this course, or where it is necessary to replace the element inherent in the roadmeter. In other words, this amendment, as a rule, prohibits the replacement of a roadmeter. The only possible situations are, I would say, exceptions to this principle, namely the damage to the meter, which prevents its further correct operation, or the need to replace the vehicle element. This design shall also regulate the behaviour of the owner of the motor vehicle or its holder in such a way that it is to be submitted within 10 days of the date of replacement by the new roadmeter together with the unit of measurement for reading the indication. The project creates not only obligations to owners and owners, but also, as I mentioned, to other entities. In particular, it is appropriate to indicate here the vehicle inspection stations and those employed in those stations of authorised diagnostics. These new tasks, which include the adopted regulation, are the following: firstly, the acceptance of the toll and the registration fee for the operation, fourth, the transmission to the central register of vehicles of the date, the reason for the replacement of the toll and the indication of the toll, together with the unit of measurement in the case of a vehicle not registered in Poland, and in the event of difficulties which I have already mentioned in the reading of the indication of the tollmeter, can be carried out after presentation of the opinion of the car assessor including a finding which could not be carried out in a precise manner by a diagnosis. As regards the rules in the Penal Code, which I have already mentioned here, this part of the amendment includes the Penalisation of certain behaviours aimed at unauthorised and unjustified interference in the indication of a roadmeter which have been found to require criminalisation due to their considerable degree of criminality. Here's a revision of one of the criminal code laws. The amendments proposed in this section provide for the criminalisation of acts which, firstly, consist in changing the indications of the mechanical vehicle, secondly, interfering in the correctness of the measurement of that roadmeter, thirdly, by ordering another person to modify the indications of the mechanical vehicle or interference in the correctness of the measurement of such a roadmeter. In conclusion, I would first of all like to thank the Ministry of Justice once again for piloting this important government initiative, which is a very concrete response to this margin, which is an undoubted path that worsens legal certainty and is accompanied by unauthorised interference with motor vehicles, which involves making changes to the meter indications. Secondly, this regulation corresponds to European legislation, I mentioned here the 2014 Directive of the European Parliament and of the Council. It can be said in a very general way that, following the introduction of this regulation, the issue of unauthorised interference which has been carried out in an unlawful manner, firstly, will be pursued more effectively in the Penal Code, and secondly, certainly such a situation will constitute an important legal defect, in general, preventing the proper registration of the vehicle from being properly registered in the event of its sale. I would also like to thank all parliamentary clubs for their active work on this report. I would also like to thank the representatives of the Legislative Office. So much for me, Marshal. Thank you for your attention. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2019-03-13-sejm-78-1.u220	Thank you very much. Mrs. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! I am pleased to present the position of the Poselski Club on the government draft law amending the Law on Traffic Law and the Penal Code Act, print no. 2878 and 380. High Izbo! The bill deals with an extremely important problem from the point of view of the citizen, which is to change the indication of the road-meter or even to change the road-meter itself in order to forge the true course of the vehicle. Unfortunately, this has become a common practice, extremely burdensome for people who want to buy a car. As a result, it's hard to have ensure that the indication of the track gauge reflects the actual mileage of the vehicle. Therefore, it is difficult for buyers to assess the actual technical condition and the exploitation of such a vehicle. This is why buyers are often affected by such a crime. Of course, this involves a financial loss. In a number of cases, this is an extremely painful loss, as it concerns savings often accumulated over a period of several months. The explanatory memorandum to the project states that, in the current legal state, the behaviour of falsifying the indication of the road-meter is considered to be one of the elements constituting the execution of the signs of a criminal offence, the fraud that I have referred to earlier in the article. 286 § 1 of the Penal Code. In order for this crime to occur, it is necessary to lead another person to an adverse regulation on his or her own or on another person's property by misleading him or making use of an error or inability to properly understand the action taken, the perpetrator of which must act in order to achieve the property benefit. It should therefore be pointed out that the practice commonly known as the filming of meters, the purpose of which is to forge the actual course of the vehicle, exhausts the signs of the crime. However, in practice, as also stated in the explanatory memorandum, it is difficult to prove the perpetrator during the criminal process to prove the marks of the subject of fraud of the indications of the road-meter, as it is difficult to identify the perpetrator. Therefore, the legislative initiative to close this gap must be taken and our debate here must be assessed positively. Ladies and gentlemen! I'm getting to the details. It should be noted that the bill introduces a number of amendments. First of all, the amendment in principle prohibits the replacement of the tracker. An exception to this rule will be the situation of damage to the tracker and its replacement in order to install a new, efficient one. The roadmeter may be replaced by one which is appropriate, dedicated to the vehicle concerned. Secondly, the project includes the penalization of interference in the direction of the tracker. They will be punished, among others. a change in the indication of the traffic meter or an order for action to be taken by another person. Therefore, the criminal liability will cover both the owner and the person actually involved in the manipulation. In addition, the authorised control authorities will be able to read the course and introduce it into the IT system. Data collected in the ICT system will be available to citizens, which will contribute to reducing the risk at the time of purchase of the vehicle. Will this completely solve the problem of manipulating the meter indication? In my opinion - and I have no doubts here - this will not completely solve the problem. This type of regulation is in force in many countries and, as the evaluations show, unfortunately this problem of fraud still exists. In addition, there are doubts about the imposition of new obligations e.g. to vehicle control stations without introducing a regulation to cover the costs of additional work at the same time. These costs are already transferred to entrepreneurs in the next law. Let me remind you that we have recently managed to stop such a very dangerous industry, but also with regard to road safety, changes in the area of vehicle control. Then, during the debate, many bitter words were uttered in connection with such, I would say, difficult operation of vehicle control stations. After all, this is important for road safety. The bill doesn't change anything here, and that's also a problem. There is a question of historic vehicles which are not included in this Act, and here also the previous speakers pointed out that in this case the issues related to the road map are not obvious. On the other hand, in general, as far as this law is concerned, this social area is important and it must be assessed positively - but it does not solve the problem in a comprehensive way, we find it a very serious charge. Taking into account [[♪ The bell ♪]] this social aspect, the Members' Club Modern is willing to support this law, but we will put forward a final position after examining the amendments tabled during the debate. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2019-03-13-sejm-78-1.u238	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Prime Minister! Allow me to present, because I have a time limit of 15 minutes, the most important elements of the annual information adopted by the General Assembly of Judges of the Constitutional Tribunal. This concerns the year 2017. In 2017, the Court issued a total of 408 judgments in substantive proceedings, including 36 judgments, 53 orders to terminate the proceedings, one signaling order, 178 provisions to refuse to grant the application to the public in full or in part, 98 provisions not to make a complaint against those provisions. All decisions of the Constitutional Tribunal shall contain justifications. In addition, the Court arranged 496 so-called. apparent complaints, in addition to the formal procedure, providing detailed answers in cases relating to citizens' complaints against judicial decisions, administrative decisions caused e.g. the long-term nature of proceedings resulting from a sense of injustice and powerlessness towards the actions of the judicial authorities and bodies. These are also complaints about social exclusion. In 2017, a total of 285 applications, legal questions and constitutional complaints were received by the Court. The Court has arranged, among others, for the Court of Justice. cases from previous years: 7 cases from 2013, 10 cases from 2014, 32 cases from 2015, 28 cases from 2016. In 2017, the Court identified two cases in the framework of preventive control. One concerned the failure to comply with the standards of the legislative procedure for the examination by the Sejm of the Senate amendments. The second case in question concerned one of the fundamental freedoms of man in a democratic state, namely freedom of assembly. At this point, let me remind you of a number of important issues relating to the functioning of the Constitutional Tribunal in 2017 compared to the previous years, which directly concerned or had an impact on the Court's judicial activities. Well, in previous years, as you remember, because I talked about it often, it was customary for judges to accumulate vacations and then pay high cash equivalents. Of course, this did not apply to all judges, for example. Ms Ewa Łętowska, at her own request for almost six months before the end of her term of office, took a leave of absence. A record player who took over 100,000. Zł, former President of the Constitutional Tribunal, Mr Andrzej Rzepliński. And for the then vice president Stanisław Biernat, over 180 thousand were planned to pay. zł, therefore, it was necessary for him to take leave before the end of his term of office. It turned out to be necessary to give all the judges late vacations. These were on average vacations of several dozen days, from 116 days of vacation to 56 days. The need for late leave by judges has been directly translated into the judicial activities of the Court. The statistics quoted by the media, which do not take this context into account, do not reliably inform the public of the actual judicial work of the judges of the Tribunal. The judges, ruling in three-and-a-half-members and full-members, issued, each of them, from 61 to 11 decisions terminating the proceedings. Differences in the case-law statistics of individual judges also result from the fact that not all judges have been adjudicated throughout the year. In 2017, two judges died during their term of office - Judge Lech Morawski in July 2017 and Judge Henryk Cioch in December 2017. Three judges - Judge Grzegorz Jędrejek, Judge Justyn Piskorski and Judge Andrzej Zielecki - began their term of office. In 2017, a statutory reorganisation was carried out, i.e. the liquidation of the Office of the Constitutional Tribunal in which the Registry of the Constitutional Tribunal and the Office of the Legal Service of the Constitutional Tribunal were established. In 2017, we no longer concluded contracts for work with persons not employed in the Constitutional Tribunal - for the preparation of a draft judgment with justification. Such practices, as you remember, have occurred in previous years. It was also necessary to restore the application of the rule of separate signature of the operative part of the order and its justification. This obvious and standard institution, not only in Poland but more widely, in the European circle of legal culture, was not used in the activities of the tribunal. Sentences of the provisions in the proceedings in the so-called. prior checking as well as the termination provisions have not been signed. Only their justification was signed. This practice had to be terminated immediately. The year 2017 was the year of stabilisation of the Court. On the one hand, this was supported by the normative peace of mind as regards the rules governing the proceedings before the Court. On the other hand, the Court's decisions have also been affected by the stability of its functioning. For example, in its judgment of 24 October 2017, the Court confirmed that the judge of the Tribunal, elected by the Sejm, who has taken the oath to the President, takes the judicial responsibility and that it is the duty of the President of the Constitutional Tribunal to assign him cases. The judge is independent in the performance of his duties and is subject only to the Constitution. In its judgment of 11 September 2017, the Court of Justice held that the appointment of the President of the Constitutional Tribunal and the Vice-President of the Constitutional Tribunal from among the candidates presented by the General Assembly of Judges of the Constitutional Tribunal constituted the prerogatives of the President, which are not subject to the control of the general courts and the Supreme Court. [[Member Kamila Gasiuk-Pihowicz: That is illegal.]] The resolution of the above problems, which have so far polarised public opinion, has enabled the Court to work normally and to take action in resolving matters that are important to citizens. It is worth noting that most of the judgments in 2017 were filed in proceedings initiated by constitutional complaints. In 2017, the Court reminded the legislators of the need to protect weaker parties. The Court recalls that these entities are workers who, by nature, have a weaker position vis-à-vis their employer, families in difficult material situations, in particular large children and underprivileged ones, as well as those at risk of homelessness. In its judgment of 18 October 2017, the Court stressed that the right to housing, although belonging to economic and social rights, is undoubtedly linked to respect for the principle of human dignity. A man deprived of an apartment, sufficient resources to buy food or treatment, has no chance of a decent life. For this reason, the Court has ruled that certain provisions on enforcement in the administration are non-constitutional because there are no regulations guaranteeing at least minimum protection against homelessness for persons who are unable to meet their own housing needs. It was a 2015 case. The Court asked the competent public authorities to pay particular attention to the needs of persons with disabilities and their carers. In its communication order of October 2017, the Court made comments to the Sejm on the appropriateness of legislative action aimed at regulating the reimbursement of the cost of travelling for disabled pupils and carers to educational establishments. The provisions in force allowed the reimbursement rates of carriage to be fixed according to different criteria and consequently at different levels, sometimes at such a low level, that the amount of the refund was so significantly different from the costs actually incurred by the parents that it limited the substance of the right to reimbursement granted to the parents. From a constitutional point of view, it is necessary to equalise educational opportunities in such a way as to ensure equal access to such forms of assistance by public authorities to all actors. The Court stressed the need to fully empower the individual in the proceedings before the judicial authorities, in particular if there are issues relating to the restriction of freedom. In its judgment of March 2017, it ruled that the applicant's right to participate in a court hearing on the enforcement of a safeguard measure was not to be deprived of its right to remain in a closed psychiatric facility. The Court reminded the legislator that personal freedom is the foundation of constitutional freedoms, the restriction of which must be subject to thorough judicial review. This check must provide for the possibility of the person concerned being actively involved in it. The Court stressed the particular importance of human freedom and personal immunity. In order to ensure the proper protection of the individual against the arbitraryness of the authority, in its judgment of 14 December 2017, he ordered the legislator to amend the rules governing the search of persons, to carry out personal checks and to search vehicles. In questioning the insufficient precision of these provisions, the Court postponed the loss of their existing force, giving the legislator a time limit for the necessary amendments. At the same time, the Court has obliged the legislator to ensure appropriate judicial review mechanisms for the application of these measures. The Court recalled the importance of citizens' confidence in the state and the law. The essence of this principle is to provide citizens with a stable legal environment, security based on legal certainty, allowing them to plan their own actions wisely and to anticipate the actions of state authorities. In its judgment of 14 December 2017, the Court contested the levying of fees for the issuance of a new driving licence as a result of a change in the data contained therein due to the operation of local authorities. According to the Court, if the need to exchange such documents is not a consequence In accordance with the principle of state loyalty to citizens, these fees should not be charged to their holders. In that judgment, the Tribunal also required the legislator to review the legal system, taking into account the existence of other similar rules, with a view to their immediate collective amendment. The judgments presented do not, of course, cover a rich spectrum of issues relevant to citizens and the state as their community, which was resolved by the Court in 2017. However, they clearly show that, contrary to the alleged allegations, the tribunal does not only act properly, but also ensures that its case-law protects the freedoms and constitutional rights of the citizens of the Republic of Poland and the proper functioning of the authorities of the State. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2019-03-13-sejm-78-1.u286	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, Law and Justice, I present my position on the draft law amending the law on combatants and certain victims of war and post-war repression and certain other laws, prints Nos 428, 1888, 2523, 2671 and 3279. The main objectives of this project are as follows. The first objective is to make a number of changes concerning updating, clarifying, deleting non-essential provisions and simplifying procedures for the recognition of applications for the status of combatant and victim of repression and to enable administrative proceedings to resume in the event of the emergence of new documents. The next objective of the Act is to help the disabled with their allowance for invalidity pensions and the possibility for pension authorities to provide benefits to the disabled living abroad. The next objective is to expand the list of powers for politically repressed soldiers in 1949 and 1959 who were forced to work in coal mines, quarryings, uranium ore plants and construction battalions. Another important issue is the monetary assistance of the Head of the Office for Combatants and Persons Repressed for deported to forced labour and imprisoned in labour camps by the authorities of the Third Reich and the USSR, who are in a difficult material situation. Another important issue is to allow the civilian blind victims of war activities in difficulty to receive financial assistance from the Head of the Office for Combatants and Repressed Persons and to grant them a lump sum of energy. Another issue is the coincidence of benefits for entitled persons and the processing of personal data. Another issue is to deform the recovery of the books of the war invalid for all those veterans who have been issued the legitimacy of the oppressed as a result of misinterpretation of the rules. The last regulated issue is the right to a parking card for veterans fighting for independence, which results from the state's obligation to provide special care to those who, with weapons in hand, risking their lives and health, defended the independence and territorial integrity of the Republic of Poland. It is also reasonable to extend the right to this parking card to those who drive veterans. Law and Justice supports the bill and will vote in favour of it. Mr. Marshal! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, I propose amendments. [[Applause]]

Our history, especially the last two hundred and several decades, has marked the tragic impact on the lives of many generations of our citizens. The last century of regained independence is also a time when independence had to be fought for many times, and at the same time many were wronged by the power. These people must always be remembered and provided with constant support, and solutions should be adapted and updated to the needs of those to be served, as well as to the growing opportunities of the state. To the extent that assistance to combatants and oppressed persons is increased and the rules and procedures applied are simplified and consistent with other aid schemes, e.g. as regards health services or the care system for independent persons, we support the new regulations introduced. At this stage of the work, we are proposing amendments on two main issues: on solutions addressed to miners' soldiers and the whole circle of people similarly repressed until 1959, as well as on rules and procedures for allocating documents confirming rights to combatants, which are identical to those granted to disabled persons on the basis of a parking card. As regards the miners' soldiers and those repressed until 1959, we believe that solutions should be put in place, which the Civic Platform Parliamentary Club - the Civic Coalition proposed more than 3 years ago, in a bill submitted to the Marshal of the Sejm at the end of 2015, which was given a print number in March 2016, 3 years ago. [[And eight years wasn't enough?]] We believe that there is no other justification today, separate, which is worse, for the treatment of miners' soldiers than other people being oppressed. We believe that the easiest solution, which will end with worse treatment of these people, will be to include miners' soldiers in the law on combatants and repressed persons. These persons should receive the same benefits and have the same powers as other persons being repressed. This is the first package of our amendments. The second objective is to introduce the same rules as for disabled persons for granting permissions to use a mobile parking card. We believe that many practical experiences, presented especially by the police, but also by organisations of persons with disabilities, indicate that this mode, which has been applied to disabled persons when issuing a parking card, should also be applied, if the same, the same powers are granted to other persons, in this case to combatants. After all, the point is that those who receive this right should be able to exercise this right in real terms to limit it, because they can never be eliminated completely, situations where such powers are exercised by those who do not need it and who, without proper authority, abuse someone's parking card. And that is why I am proposing a second package of amendments, which concerns precisely this issue, in order, firstly, to issue these cards for a fixed period of not more than 5 years, so that, secondly, those who receive such a right declare that they do not have a regular parking card so as not to issue double parking cards, because then it is clear that this second parking card can serve a parallel circulation and, finally, that, thirdly, this combatant's parking card is easy to identify for the police and other control services, whether such a right is actually exercised by the person to whom it belongs. This is the case with two sets of amendments which I am sending through the secretariat of the meetings. Thank you.

The basis of the State's action is to provide reliable care to persons who have been awarded outstanding activities and even have been able to give their lives in the name of the functioning of the independent Republic of Poland. It welcomes the approach in which we significantly extend the scope of certain provisions, such as: to take into account the health situation of combatants. The rules extend aid to living veterans and provide real support in their daily lives in the country for which they have devoted their health. It is also a support for families of those who have fallen in the name of safe Poland. We welcome the proposal for the wording of the article. 12a Act on the supply of war and military invalids and their families, thanks to which an allowance of PLN 850 will be payable to the invalidity pension. Every zloty of support for these people is a duty of the state and we have no doubt that this is a right and necessary change. However, we are not able to go past some of the inconsistencies contained in the draft subcommittee law, which I would like to emphasise in my speech. All these projects relate to combatants and victims of war and post-war repression. It is worth noting that it is important to honor these people and their surroundings with special care from the state because of their contribution to regaining independence and fighting for free Poland. And while those who were being repressed were directly in the territory of the Republic of Poland or were being exported from the territory of the country to places of isolation in the territories of the Soviet Union, war veterans - like the present veterans - served the Polish people outside the borders, taking part in e.g. In the fights at Monte Cassino or in the Middle East. On the one hand - what is important and worth paying attention here - we are dealing with people who are leaving this world before our eyes, and on the other hand, when it comes to the supply of foreign mission veterans, it is still stored in the seym freezer. We give powers to combatants and repressed people, for example. in Article 4, paragraph 1 is replaced by the following: Article 13 (1) (b) and (c) of Regulation (EU) No 1303/2013 Point 3 (1), which reads: periodic monetary assistance may be intended in particular to meet living needs and to protect health in the case of a long-term disease resulting in an increase in the cost of living, buying medicines, dressings and commuting to medical and rehabilitation facilities. So benefits can be granted regardless of the cause of the disease. This depends mainly on the living needs and costs of this disease. However, assistance to foreign mission veterans depends on the health damage caused by the activities carried out in the course of the tasks. Moreover, in the following section, we read that: periodic monetary aid may be used in particular to pay for the care necessary for age and health. On the other hand, we limit access to this assistance to service veterans outside the country, making it dependent on the degree of their disability, not e.g. from general health. Calculations in Article The 18th draft law, which was drafted by the subcommittee, is incomprehensible. In 2020 there is a decrease of 30,000. PLN compared to 2019, by 10,000 in 2021. zł compared to the previous year. In the following years, the fall below the set limit no longer occurs, which is not understandable between 2021 and 2028. Especially since, for example, in the following points concerning the Social Security Office, the CRUS, the Pension Office of the Prison Service or the Pension and Pension Department of the Ministry of Internal Affairs and Administration, this decrease is visible in different years and is not logically arranged. Both veterans or repressed persons and foreign mission veterans in draft laws should be treated as a single recipient. The way in which the privileges of all these groups are honoured should be harmonised. It must be taken into account that all these people have contributed and contributed to the freedom and sovereignty of Poland and the security of its citizens. Both combatants and foreign mission veterans fought not only in the country but also in many parts of the world. Repressed people fought for democratic changes in Poland as they did and did foreign missions veterans in other countries around the world. Disabled combatants and veterans injured during foreign missions face similar or the same health problems. Both veterans' widows or those who were repressed and soldiers' widows who took part in foreign missions buried men who sacrificed their lives for their homeland. Special support is needed not only for the widows of those soldiers who are often middle-aged, but also for their parents and children. Another problem that exists among widows is the problem of getting a pension from a fallen soldier. [[This isn't about...]] I know, I know. This witness can be obtained only after many criteria have been met, among others. Proving that death was caused by a criminal act, which is often impossible because of difficulties in carrying out a full investigation by the prosecution. As a result, the only means to sustain the fallen soldier's family during a foreign mission are often the widow's income from employment in a military unit. I also have a question about the legislative process. Why are we discussing these changes so late? The projects were presented a few years ago and were now remembered. What made them decide to work on them at this very moment? We can only guess. Finally, I would like to highlight one more point, particularly in the context of the government's current historical policy. Why Article 1 is repealed from the law on combatants and certain victims of war repression and post-war period. 1 paragraph 2 item 2, which says that it is considered as a combatant activity to participate in Polish military formations and organizations in the First World War, in national uprisings and fights for the recovery or maintenance of territories of the Republic of Poland? As a result of this change we deprive the honorary title of the veteran of all those who died who gave their lives in the fight for their homeland during World War I, all national uprisings and fights for regaining the territory of the Republic of Poland or maintaining its coherence. They are dead, they are no longer among us, but symbolic deprivation of their title can be taken as an insult to their descendants, and above all to free Poland, for which they gave their lives. This gesture undoubtedly hits the memory of heroes who can no longer speak on their behalf. It is our duty to remind them of their deeds in their name. The draft law of the subcommittee is needed, although, as you can see, there are also content for which we need clarification. However, there is no doubt that the state's social assistance should be provided to combatants or their families as a gesture of respect for those who have sacrificed their health or life in the name of Poland's security and sovereignty. Our duty as representatives of the nation is to care for them and provide them with decent living conditions. The Modern Club will vote in favour of the draft law prepared by the Permanent Sub-Committee on Social Policy. Thank you. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2019-03-13-sejm-78-1.u316	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! In most European countries, there are laws that provide for the special care of veterans of wars waged by these countries and those persecuted. However, the scope of these benefits is different in each country. As a rule, these are cash benefits and various types of relief and facilitation. Thus, although, in principle, the proposed solutions are common on a European scale, they do not have their counterparts in the legislation of other countries in detail. Nevertheless, the Constitution of the Republic of Poland clearly indicates that the natural and inalienable dignity of man is inviolable, and its respect and protection are the responsibility of public authorities. Therefore, the project that we are currently dealing with is very important. It concerns the aid provided for those who once put their health and their lives at risk, fighting for free Poland. Today they often need the care of the state and they are not in a position to finance the care they need. Today, we face a number of fundamental challenges with regard to the revision of the rules governing the powers of veterans. These include, in particular, updating and clarifying the rules, removing unused rules and introducing a number of simplifications. The aim is to simplify procedures for the recognition of applications for the status of combatant and victim of repression, to allow the renewal of administrative proceedings in the event of the emergence of new documents on matters within longer periods than provided for in the Code of Administrative Procedure. The regulations deal with the issue of assisting the disabled by granting them the right to the allowance of a pension to an invalidity pension and introducing the possibility for pension bodies to provide benefits to disabled people living abroad, to extend the catalogue of rights granted by the authorities of the Third Reich and the USSR, who are in a difficult material situation, to receive financial assistance from the Head of the Office for Combatants and Workers, finally to allow the civilian victims of war, who are in a difficult material situation, to receive financial assistance from the Head of the Office for Combatants and the Repressed Persons, and to grant them additional benefits - a lump of energy, as well as to regulate issues related to the co-operation of anti-communist opposition activists and those repressed for these reasons. It is also important to simplify procedures and thus to allow the recovery of the books of the invalid war to all those veterans who have been issued the legitimacy of the oppressed as a result of misinterpretation of the rules. In our view, the changes are fully justified and forced over time, which is why the PSL Club will support these proposals. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-PL_2019-03-13-sejm-78-1.u322	Dear Marshal, High Seym! I have the honour on behalf of the Civic Platform - Civic Coalition Parliamentary Club to present my position on amendments to the Rules of Procedure of the Sejm, contained in print No 3181. Three amendments are included in this draft resolution. First, technical, changing name: General Data Protection Officer to: President of the Office for Personal Data Protection. This is an obvious change that is beyond doubt. The second amendment states that the Sejm Office will draw up records of the subcommittee meetings, which will later be included in the Sejm Information System. For many years now, the subcommittee's meetings have been recorded for several years. This is a reminder of the fact that it was difficult to find out who had tabled an amendment at the subcommittee meeting years ago, today a symbolic, legendary: or journals. And since then, the subcommittee meetings have been recorded so that the transparency of the legislative process can be maintained. On the other hand, there are no records made out of them, and they are not made available. Of course, the transparency of public life, the transparency of the legislative process is an important issue, even if we have to expect it to cost money. Because, given even the reality of last year, when costs amounted to about PLN 0.5 million, and the subcommittees did not work much, because the draft laws go to the Sejm and are adopted by committees instantly, you can expect that these costs will be higher. But of course, that doesn't raise our doubts. The third change, which speaks of proceding, information, reports or reports to the Sejm, which includes operational and cognitive activities, raises doubts. The Rules of Procedure of the Sejm state precisely how the Sejm deals with reports and information submitted by the Supreme Chamber of Control, the National Council of Radiophony and Television, the Constitutional Tribunal, the President of the Supreme Court, the National Council of Courts, the Ombudsman of the rights of the child or the President of the NBP, and in general mention that in the case of other information, reports and reports, the Sejm may review them, and all these reports and reports shall be delivered to Members. The proposed amendment states that if the information of other institutions, not specifically mentioned earlier, contains records resulting from operational and exploratory activities, they will be sent to the Commission for Special Services and will therefore not be made available to other Members, and thus the above mentioned provision will not be operational. There is no explanation of the origin of this amendment in the justification for the draft resolution, which is laconic. It was presented by the President of the Committee on Special Services at a committee meeting. Mr President drew attention to the interesting problem that, as far as I am aware, he has been sent to the Marshal of the Sejm for two years the information of the head of the National Tax Administration, which contains this information, saying in quotes, the tax intelligence resulting from the operation and diagnosis, and this information is not directed by the Marshal of the Sejm to any committee. It is not addressed to the Committee on Public Finance, as it has a strictly secret clause, nor is it addressed to the Committee on Special Services, which does not have the powers laid down in the Rules of Procedure. While appreciating the Head of the Special Services Commission for paying attention to an important problem, we are, however, expressing doubts about this change for two reasons. Firstly, it is possible to express, in the experience of this term of office, the concern that this provision will not be able to cover up various information and reports, since it can be concluded that this information was based on operational and operational operations, that it would be classified in a strictly confidential way and would then only be sent to the Commission for Special Services. However, even rejecting this concern, it seems that the Commission for Special Services does not seem appropriate to analyse the information of the head of the National Tax Administration. Perhaps another solution should be found, perhaps within the Committee on Public Finance, to set up a subcommittee, perhaps to recommend that the head of the institution should create information that does not reveal details of intelligence, because it seems that operational information in this report is nevertheless in minority, and there is valuable information on which the Committee on Public Finance should lean. Therefore, Mr Marshal, on behalf of the club, I wanted to make amendments to this draft resolution aimed at deleting these entries, deleting points 2 and lit. and from point 8, that is to say, the deletion of this area, where it is said that reports containing intelligence will only be sent to the Commission for Special Services. Thank you. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2019-03-13-sejm-78-1.u330	Dear Marshal, High Izbo! On behalf of the Law and Justice Club, I have the honour to present my position on the government draft law amending the Public Roads Act, print No. 3259. The amendment of the Act aims to continue the assumptions adopted in the Act of 8 December 2017 amending the Public Road Act in which the right to collect electronic tolls and tolls by highway to the main road transport inspector is entrusted with the concentration of all competences relating to the National toll collection system within the government administration body. The national toll collection system plays a very important role in the transport policy of the State. This is a modern, transparent and efficient tool for implementing transport policy and transport policy at the level of individual carriers. [[Introduced by the Platform?]] The primary role of the system is to generate revenue for the National Road Fund, from which investments on national roads are financed, and to ensure that the European Union budget absorbs resources in the current and future financial perspective. As it was considered necessary to ensure adequate exchange of certain data with other public entities during the implementation of the new National Pay Collection System project, e.g. with the National Tax Administration, it is also proposed to amend the rules aimed at carrying out activities related to electronic toll collection systems by the designated entity. The draft provides for a provision authorising the Minister responsible for. transport for the issue of a regulation in which it designates an entity for the benefit of the above mentioned. services and the entity will have exclusive right to perform the services indicated in the draft law and detailed in the regulation issued under it. The following provisions also govern the provisions of the service contract concluded between the Central Road Transport Inspectorate and the designated entity. What is important, according to the earlier provisions of the Law of 8 December 2017, the continued operation of the system remains the task of the Chief Inspector of Road Transport and therefore the Act does not have any additional consequences for the state administration collecting different data, which in fact makes it easier to exchange them. Therefore, the Law and Justice Club will support changes aimed at, inter alia, to ensure the development and efficiency of the National Payment Collection System, to optimise the cost of the system in the long term, to improve standardisation and ICT solutions, to ensure the proper protection of data collected within the system and to improve the transmission of data to the relevant services. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]

ParlaMint-PL_2019-03-13-sejm-78-1.u351	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I am pleased to present to you today a project closing the package of 100 changes, something else in the Ministry of Development we called a package of 100 changes for companies. It is a draft amendment to the Commercial Companies Code, often commonly referred to as a project with a simple public limited company. [[Game in the O.R.]] Before I begin to describe the solutions contained in the project... [[Excuse me, Minister. Ladies and gentlemen, I would very much like you to transfer your calls. Thank you very much.]] Undersecretary of State at the Ministry of Entrepreneurship and Technology Marek Nieżak: Before I begin to describe the solutions contained in the project, I would like to emphasise something else first. This project, as it happens, did not have its beginning in the heads of officials, nor in the heads, I don't know, lawyers, law professors, merchants, just a signal or impulse to create it, came out of the start-up environment. This is, of course, a young environment and not so large on the scale of the Polish economy, but it is extremely important both from the perspective of the agenda of the Ministry of Entrepreneurship and Technology and the entire government. In 2016, it was estimated that if we create favourable conditions for the development of start-ups as a country, this sector could generate over PLN 2 billion of added value for the economy by 2023 and create about 50,000. jobs. According to the report "Polish start-ups 2018" more than 78% respondents from the start-up environment who were involved in the drafting of this report indicated that there was a need to adapt capital requirements, requirements for the creation, operation of capital companies to the precisely designed, simplified form of corporate vehicle and the form of a simple public limited liability company. This is extremely important, in my opinion, because it shows that this project is the answer to a very specific need for a very specific group, i.e. It's a start-up environment. In this context, it is also worth mentioning that the Polish Commercial Companies Code was established almost 20 years ago and is largely based on solutions adopted in the old pre-war Allerhand Code of the 1930s. Many of the structures contained there are, still very good, but they are suitable for, say, typical, repetitive business models. He is often very versatile and does not necessarily meet the needs of dynamically developing start-ups. It should be borne in mind that they are companies that are often very fast and easily formed on the basis of some idea, idea, often do not have their own capital, and at the same time it must be remembered that very often they also end up without much success. It is estimated that about 9 out of 10 folds, so to speak, without success, and this is also a very important element, which in some way was tried to take into account in the project presented. Also so-called. The White Paper on Innovation, which has been conducted in our Ministry since 2016, an analysis, consultation with entrepreneurs, representatives of the science world and practices confirmed this diagnosis that start-ups need a new type of corporate vehicle, and the answer to this is a simple public limited company. I would also like to stress that it is not such that this is a specific Polish idea. On the contrary. All over Europe, individual countries are creating new legal solutions for innovative companies. As examples, I can give the Slovak simple public limited company or the French simplified public limited company, which is one of the most popular legal forms in France. Capital requirements are also falling in many European countries, EU countries. This is precisely the form of simplification, which we also envisage in this project. The simple public limited company we propose is based on two basic assumptions. Firstly, simplicity in operation, in principle, in action, or liquidation. We have limited formal requirements to the minimum in each of the areas I mentioned. The second assumption is maximum flexibility. The proposed rules do not restrict freedom where this is not necessary, in particular because of the safety of trading, which is also very much at our heart, as well as the protection of creditors and minority shareholders. Now I'm going to come up with a little more detailed basic, basic solutions. In the case of a simple joint stock company, we do not have formal or financial barriers at the start. The company can be opened in 24 hours with share capital of PLN 1. The capital structure is based on completely new assumptions for Polish law. Permanent share capital will not necessarily be created. Change in capital Nor will it in principle require the amendment of the company's contract or the completion of any formal procedures for the increase or reduction of capital. The company's assets will be easily available, simply adapting it to the current needs that can happen - such is the specificity of start-ups - very rapidly change. At the same time, we have ensured that this is accompanied by a whole set of mechanisms which, more effectively than traditionally understood share capital, safeguard the interests of the company's creditors. The amount of the funds that the company must maintain will not be expressed in the arbitrary amount of share capital but will be adjusted to the amount of the company's liabilities and the requirement to maintain its solvency. In contrast to other equity companies in PSA, the distribution to shareholders, e.g. By way of dividend, it will only be possible if this does not jeopardise the solvency of the company. It is a mechanism that seems more effective in securing the interests of creditors than this traditional share capital. Both the recipient of the amount paid and all members of the bodies will be jointly and severally liable for its reimbursement if the payment violates the company's rules or contract. In addition, as equity, the company will have to maintain a minimum reserve of 5%. the amounts of commitments. PSA will therefore be the only capital company in which the equity capital will be linked to the liquidity requirement and the current size of the liabilities. PSA shares - this is also an extremely important novum in relation to already existing SA and limited liability companies - will be available for work or services. This is particularly important for these young, innovative companies, where, as I have already said, very often this capital, which is owned by young or beginner shareholders, is primarily the capital of their knowledge, the capital of their ideas, the capital of their work or know-how. This is, as I said, unacceptable in other equity companies. As a result, the founders will be able to maintain their influence on decisions in the company. Investors will be assured that the key idea and human capital in this company will remain. Thanks. Founding shares may also be spared from losing their voting power in the company at the time of the search for capital. This is also extremely important, because I would like to point out that today such a problem that we see on the market start-ups or challenges - maybe this is a better term - it is that capital, venture capital funds are often not Polish and as a requirement they make de facto takeover... By increasing the capital, they take control of the company, sometimes force the registration of the company outside Poland, and that is what we want to avoid, this is what we want to avoid. We want to keep these start-ups in our country and this, among others. is intended to serve a new corporate form. PSA shares will also be dematerialised in a simplified register, which can be maintained e.g. by an investment firm or notary. The register will be able to be kept using blockchain technology, which is consistent with the latest trends and, I think, makes PSA one of the most modern legal forms in Europe. At the same time PSA will have no public company responsibilities and its shares will not be subject to organised trading. Thus, we retain the flexibility associated with the fact that we do not operate on the basis of the rules on which public companies operate, public companies, while ensuring transparency and security of trading in shares, while minimizing costs. The maximum simplification also includes requirements for PSA bodies. And here is also a very interesting novel, i.e. Of course, it is possible to appoint a board of directors and even a board of directors. That's what it looks like in other companies. However, we also allow the creation of a board of directors in PSA instead of a board of directors, which is in line with the Anglo-Saxon model and, it seems, can encourage investment in Anglo-Saxon capital from the United States or England. The idea is that such a company registered in Poland should operate in a more well-known way to Anglo-Saxon capital, in a way that is more understandable and transparent there, which may make investment decisions easier, which is what we want, because in fact it is also about facilitating start-ups of development by raising capital. The PSA will be able to use electronic communications to a very wide extent to adopt resolutions and hold meetings, which should make decision-making processes much easier and faster. The project also envisages accelerating the liquidation of the company. As I said before, the fact that 9 out of 10 start-ups do not end in success, of course can be a little less optimistic, but the starting-up philosophy is a little bit about not to worry and move on, it is about getting over failure quickly. The necessary element here is also the possibility of a rapid liquidation of the company, and this is ensured by PSA: shortening winding-up dates compared to the liquidation of other capital companies and simplifying the termination of the business. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Given the above and, above all, the desire to improve the regulatory environment for the functioning of Polish companies, especially young and innovative ones, we kindly ask you to adopt a law amending the Code of Commercial Companies Act. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]
ParlaMint-PL_2019-03-13-sejm-78-1.u352	Thank you very much, Minister. The Sejm agreed that in the discussion on this item it would hear five minutes' statements on behalf of clubs and circles. I'm opening a discussion. Mr Marek Ast, Law and Justice have the floor.
ParlaMint-PL_2019-03-13-sejm-78-1.u353	Mr. Marshal! Minister! High Izbo! On behalf of the Parliamentary Club, Law and Justice, I am pleased to present my position on the Government's draft law amending the Commercial Companies Code Act and certain other laws, print No. 3236. The project is part of a package prepared by the Ministry of Development of 100 changes for companies, facilitations for entrepreneurs. The project proposes to regulate a new type of capital company, so-called. a simple public limited liability company. PSA is in this proposal a modern form of a non-public capital company dealing with innovative projects. It is intended to combine the corporate nature of the company as a legal person and its fundamental manifestation of the exclusion of the shareholders' personal responsibility for its obligations, with the considerable freedom to shape the company's relations in terms of both the mutual relationship between the shareholders and the company's management system and the supervision of this process. On the one hand, this new structure of the company is distinguished by the acceptance of new categories of contributions to cover the covered membership rights and on the other hand by the modern mechanism to protect the company's creditors from the construction of share capital in favour of flexible distribution restrictions for shareholders taking into account both the size of the company's aggregate debt and the degree of its solvency. The need to introduce a new type of capital company into the Polish legal order results from the analysis of the legal environment and functioning in Poland so-called. Start-ups. These are projects that have the potential for very rapid growth through technological advantage or market niche, but for which the legal forms of business activity are anachronistic. The project proposes to create a new form of commercial company instead of attempting to reform the construction of existing capital companies due to legal constraints, the extent of the necessary changes and the safety of trading. The solutions envisaged in the project are intended to provide an alternative to existing legal structures. The designer decided to do so for several reasons. Firstly, it is not possible to significantly modify the provisions on public limited liability companies, since the provisions on that company are harmonised by the relevant provisions of Union law. The introduction of the admissibility of contributions in the form of work or services or even merely giving up the formalities relating to the taking-up of shares in exchange for returns would require amendments to the relevant provisions of this law at European Union level. Therefore, changes aimed at significantly simplifying the construction of companies, in particular the reduction of formal and financial requirements for the founders of commercial companies, are being implemented in the countries of the European Union by introducing new types of companies. Secondly, the provisions on a limited liability company, which largely continue to resolve the 1934 Commercial Code, would require so far-reaching changes that it would indeed be modified in nature, and this is not the purpose of this project. For construction elements of the company z o.o. belong, inter alia, to:: share capital, i.e. fixed initial capital, the principle that equity rights are linked to share capital, the principle that shares cannot be incorporated in a document or in an electronic record. The property structure of the company o.o. - based on share capital and shares forming part of it, is not sufficiently functional for innovation and new technologies projects. In particular, the model of the company O.O. does not allow the introduction of non-nominative actions, which in turn enable contributions to be made in the form of work. The reform of a limited liability company leading to the separation of equity rights from share capital as the core capital of the limited liability company could raise doubts as it would significantly alter the nature of the limited liability company. This would in fact lead to the creation of a new type of company in place of a limited liability company. In this situation, these government proposals are moving towards the creation of a new type of company. We believe that these proposals are going [[♪ The bell ♪]] In the right direction. Therefore, on behalf of the club, I request that the draft be referred to the committee responsible for further legislative work. Thank you. [[Applause]]

I know that each of us mentally misses times like this. ancient Rome, where there was the law of ten or twelve stone tablets as a whole of the law written. Unfortunately, since then, reality has become so complicated that we won't get back to it. All important acts are long, sometimes even very long. As far as the length of regulation on a simple public limited liability company is concerned, I would like to draw attention to such an element. At some point in writing the project had to be decided, I would say, legislative, technical, how do we write: Are we writing a complete regulation or are we making references to other regulations? And, for example, French regulation - earlier I referred to it because there is such a simple company there - is much shorter, but it is based on references. So there are fewer articles in there, but that's because it also refers to the proper application of the provisions concerning this main public limited company, say, the basic company or the ordinary French public limited company. We've adopted a different concept. We have found that references will in fact make it difficult to read this regulation. And this simplicity is not that these rules are simple to understand, because they are not simple to understand. This is the Commercial Companies Code, it's not simple matter. Nevertheless, it is about simplicity of application. And the simplicity of application, I think, has been achieved precisely because of the far-reaching flexibility. The point is that shareholders who establish such a company will have a very broad spectrum of recognition as to how this company will operate. And that's the simplicity of this solution. There was also a voice that this company wouldn't bring happiness. But let me save it for last, because this is, I think, this thing, this sweet thing that's worth saving for last. This is, I think, very serious and I already know the accusation from professors criticizing the project that it is inconsistent with the basic principles of the current Commercial Companies Code. I'd say that's a slightly twisted argument, because every new thing is different from what we've had so far. If we are different, new things are treated as inconsistencies, then yes, to some extent. It's inconsistent because it's new. On the other hand, when it comes to the fact that this is contradictory or incompatible with the Commercial Companies Code, absolutely not. It's just going to be a new form, a little bit different from the O.R. company, different from the public limited companies, which is provided in the code mainly for start-ups, new start-ups, innovative companies, not so much based on traditionally understood capital, or money, or some kind of fetches, but on some ideas, some innovative ways of doing something, some inventions. A very serious question, perhaps the most serious of those who have died here at all, is whether we have mechanisms to safeguard the interests of the creditor. This also applies to the question of whether we have considered the risks or whether we have tried to take them into account. I'm going to start by saying that I'm deeply convinced that abuse happens regardless of the form in which companies operate. [[Exactly.]] Whether the Amber Gold scandal, or, I don't know, now the GetBack scandals, they... The legal form in which the company operates is not the key to these issues at all. I don't think thinking about it that way is a good direction. Moreover, already today, everyone who is on average familiar with the Commercial Companies Code is able to limit the liability of their client very much. If this one wishes, he'll make him a partnership with the O.O., a limited partnership. Many companies operate in such a form and I think that is just the reality of commercial law today. So I think this is the first point to start thinking about it at all. On the other hand, we have done a great deal to ensure that a simple public limited company protects the interests of creditors and secures their satisfaction. Let's start with that again. Today in the Commercial Companies Code required share capital for the company z o.o. There's 5,000. zł. This is a solution that already works. Tell me if you're really convinced that you're paying $5,000. zł per share capital secures the interests of the creditors of the company z o.o.? That's $5,000. zł. We're talking more serious business. I think the answer's obvious. Nor is it that we in PSA of this share capital, let it be 5,000. PLN or 100,000. zł in the case of a public limited liability company, we do not supplement anything, we do not replace this resignation. We are simply introducing other security mechanisms, and I believe that much more real than share capital. I will repeat what I said when presenting the project: the amount of funds that PSA must maintain will not be expressed in an arbitrary amount of capital. It will be adjusted to the extent of the company's current liabilities and the solvency requirement. That's the mechanism. We are not talking about formalised fixed capital, but about up-to-date capital depending on the level of the company's liabilities. It will also be possible to pay dividends in PSA only if this does not jeopardise the solvency of the company. Both the recipient of the amount paid and all members of the authorities will be liable for its reimbursement jointly and severally for their own assets if such payment violates the company's rules or contract. In addition, the company will have to maintain a minimum reserve of 5% on its share capital. the sum of commitments, I repeat, the current sum of commitments. I firmly believe that these are real mechanisms that safeguard the interests of creditors to a greater extent than share capital of 5 000. zł., which is currently required in the case of the company z o.o. There were also voices that it was, I don't know, I guess that's what I'm saying from memory, but I think so, the pride of the designer, such an excessive ambition. Why introduce a new type when you can try to reform existing ones? As regards the attempts to reform existing forms of the capital company, i.e. the public limited liability company and the O.O. company, the margin for the public limited liability company is very small. This is an area where we have very strong European regulations. We also have the possibility of public broadcasting, becoming a public company. The margin for making the classic public limited liability company more flexible is very, very small, if not none. As far as the companies are concerned, o.. Remember, this is today's most popular form of company. If we started very violently, in fact changing the essence of this form of company, we would carry out - I have the impression that such a term came from this speaker - an operation on a living organism. We'd change the system almost 300,000. companies, while in most cases they are companies with o.o. they are the most universal corporate vehicle. They're used, so to speak, in very different areas, not to say that practically everyone. It seems to me that there is no need to change the basic corporate vehicle, which in Poland is used in practically every industry to adapt to the specifics of start-ups. It would be an operation that would certainly affect hundreds of thousands of Polish entrepreneurs. It seems to me that a much safer and more sensible idea is to build a new type of company and adapt it to the needs of a new, growing industry or sector. It is also said here that it is a form that will not bring happiness to entrepreneurs. Let me put it this way: I'm just a humble lawyer, I have no ambition to bring happiness to the world. The aim of this project is certainly not to bring happiness to all professors of law. On the other hand, we are in constant contact with the start-up industry. We hope that some of its representatives will be able to invite to work in committee and subcommittee. As far as we know, they are very satisfied with this project and support it. For me personally, this is the most important determinant. If the start-up industry is satisfied with this form and will use it, it is a success for me. Thank you very much. [[Applause]]
