ParlaMint-PL_2019-03-13-sejm-78-1.u370	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I have the honour on behalf of the club of the Polish People's Party - Union of European Democrats to present our position on the government draft law amending the Code of Commercial Companies Act and some other laws, print no. 3236. The proposed law aims at strengthening the development of start-ups in Poland, increasing their competitiveness and stopping Polish ideas from exporting abroad. The project introduces a new entity - a simple public limited company into the Polish legal order. It is intended to be a modern non-public company adapted to the modern economy. PSA will combine the limited liability of shareholders for the company's obligations with full flexibility in terms of both shaping the relationship between the shareholders and the management system of such a company. It is to be deprived of excessive formalities relating to its establishment and, on the other hand, to have a modern mechanism for the protection of the company's creditors. Under the new rules PSA will not have to have a supervisory board. High Izbo! It should be acknowledged that none of the currently available types of capital companies is therefore fully suited to the needs of at least some of the modern, innovative projects. Given the fact that many companies operate in a situation of high uncertainty requiring rapid response to changes in the business environment, there is a need for regulation to ensure appropriate flexibility and freedom of action, e.g. allowing for easier release of measures which in the circumstances are not necessary for the company to achieve its objectives and to regulate its obligations, to issue shares in return for work or services or to make the organisational structure more flexible. PSA will also be able to make binding decisions via video conferencing or e-mail. A new type of company will also be able to issue shares without nominal value. The draft amendment of the law assumes a number of rights that are intended to secure creditors and shareholders at the stage of the liquidation of the company. The distribution of the assets is intended to cover all creditors known to the company and not only those who have made their claims in winding-up proceedings. A provision has also been introduced to limit the sale of the assets of the company to its owners or persons associated with them. In turn, in the event of the acquisition of the company's assets by the shareholder acquiring the decision, the court will take the decision. The Court of First Instance will also allow the acquisition of the company's assets by the acquiring shareholder at the company's request if it is likely that this will not lead to the damage to creditors or shareholders of the company. High Izbo! Human capital - innovative technological solutions at the early stages of development, innovation and entrepreneurship - is becoming increasingly important in today's economic trade. These are the characteristics that are the most important drivers of economic growth. At the same time, these characteristics of entrepreneurs are extremely difficult to accurately value, especially at the early stages of business development. According to this assumption within the framework PSA was rightly allowed to contribute in the form of work or services. Very similar solutions to those proposed in the project have been in operation in several European countries for years. For example, in Germany there is a sub-type of a limited liability company with a minimum share capital of EUR 1. It is similar in the Czech Republic, Slovakia or Finland. Without a doubt, the proposed solution will contribute to the development of start-ups which are an increasingly important part of the economy in our country. The current legal framework did not fully correspond to such companies, often hindering their development. Currently the most popular form chosen by start-ups owners is the company z o.o. The features of this form make it difficult to achieve the new goals and challenges faced by young, innovative companies in the age of increasing the importance of human capital.

ParlaMint-PL_2019-03-13-sejm-78-1.u372	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! Ladies and gentlemen! I am pleased to present the effect of many months' work at the Ministry of Justice on the reform of criminal proceedings. The aim of the project is to improve criminal proceedings, speed up this procedure, abandon unnecessary procedural formalism, make it easier for the parties involved to proceed, prevent procedural obstruction of the parties, protect the public interest more widely in the proceedings and eliminate gross errors, errors which today lead to the fact that proceedings last for years. What are the cases? I will start with a case study, differently than usual, because by the example of this disease that digests our criminal justice system, it is best to see the meaning of our changes, the purpose of these changes and the pressing need for them to be introduced. Ladies and gentlemen! Trial obstruction. In court, I will not give many different examples, I will limit myself to three, in Nowy Sącz for the 31 dates of the designated trials 21 did not take place, in Chełma for 41 dates of the trial 35 did not take place, in Mielec for 99 hearings 48 did not take place. Specific cases show that we are very often dealing with deliberate obstruction by the defendants, their defenders, who have specialized in skilfully dragging proceedings. In one of the courts, the case came into effect in November 2008, more than 260 court dates were set, most of which were not held due to the unwarranted absence of the defendant. In another court, in a simple case concerning the act of Article. 233, these are false statements, the defendant immediately before the trial date - the case has been pending since 2011 - the day before in the afternoon or even after the start of the hearing by fax, by e-mail sends another release from the medical examiner, often the same medical examiner, stating the inability to participate in the proceedings. It's still going on. The defendant uses the same obstruction tactics all the time. In another case, the defendants have repeatedly submitted certificates from the medical examiners, they are completely capable of taking part in the trial, but this only turns out to be how the court will summon the forensic experts and examine the possibility of taking part in the hearing. Finally, the last case, because such cases best show this problem, where the trial against more than 20 defendants was skillfully blocked by the obstructive actions of those accused. The certificates were based on false tissue samples from another person, in fact it was female DNA, not male DNA. The accused simulated cancer, in fact he was completely healthy, able to participate in the proceedings. The trial's been delayed for almost two years. How do we deal with this? We are introducing the possibility of proceeding in the absence of the defendant, the justified absence of the defendant. Complementing the evidence will guarantee every sick defendant and at the same time encourage him to recover, so that we will witness miraculous healings, I am firmly convinced of that. The proposed change will certainly speed up the process and finally close the gate to block the process. The second very important change is the introduction of a wider notification by fax, fax and e-mail. Currently, the letter should contain only a postal address in the form of contact with the author. How's it going in practice? In one of the cases in the district court, 120 people had to be notified, waiting for a return receipt - up to 8 weeks. In another case, in the district court, the time needed to deliver shipments is up to 2 months. 2 months of break, where nothing happens, is stagnation between the next trial dates, because we are all waiting for a return confirmation of receipt. In the 21st century, I will remind you where we all contact with fax, e-mail and modern methods of communication. In another district court, the average waiting time for a return receipt for more than 200 witnesses is 4 weeks. In the district court in Tychy a return receipt for 28 people was obtained in 10 weeks. So that this is another very important change, making it easier for the participants of the proceedings and adapting the criminal process to modern methods of communication. The third very important change is the withdrawal from the announcement of judgments and justifications, where no one is present at the hearing or at the hearing. At present, any ruling or order given, whether at the hearing or at the sitting, shall be delivered orally, of course, at a public hearing. The proposed amendment provides for the possibility to withdraw from this rigid regulation where no one is placed on the announcement. It will then be possible to declare the judgment delivered as announced and to indicate the reason for the withdrawal from the notice in the minutes. Examples of cases, please. In the first half of 2018, such a situation took place in 24 cases in one of the courts. The total time of reading documents was over 8 hours, the number of pages of judgments - 445. In Ciechanów a sentence of 117 pages was announced 13 hours and 55 minutes in an empty room. The fourth amendment is the possibility for another member of the formation to read the documents. Currently, the documents, the protocols to be read, shall be read by the chairman of the formation of the court. This is extremely incriminating and often counterproductive. The proposed amendment is intended to allow for the reading of documents and protocols by another member of the formation of the court or protocol. In one court, 300 cards were read by eight court dates, nothing else was happening, just reading the minutes. In another court of 482 the cards were read by eight court dates, in another court reading took place by 18 court dates. At this point I would like to point out to you this case, namely the case of the accused of participating in an organised criminal group, which had 248 volumes, each of 200 pages each. The judge's reading of the file began in 2009. For a year 51 volumes of files have been read. The court has scheduled another 12 time limits for the trial. During the proceedings, the accused slept and sometimes even snored, as the media pointed out. Ladies and gentlemen, we are dealing with an absolute mockery of the justice system and we must say to such situations: stop. Another example: documents were only read on 17 days' trial dates. So that another change would be precisely to consider such documents to be disclosed without reading them. After all, everyone knows them, they can get acquainted with them, the trial is open. Meanwhile, we are stuck in a system of almost 19th century, the court reads to an empty room with absolute passiveness of the person concerned, or the accused. Withdrawal from the oral report at the review hearing where only the parties to the proceedings were present. At present, regardless of the appearance of the parties and the public, the appeal court shall present an oral report. Often it is art for art, for what is there? This is very often time-consuming, the report is extensive, and there are results, the course of the proceedings so far, in particular the content of the judgment under appeal, the pleas in appeal, the justification of the pleas in appeal, the conclusions, the direction of the appeal, the scope of the appeal. The Court reads this report, which, of course, everyone can read. We have examples: 15 defendants, 15 appeals, charges on 39 pages, the hearing lasts all day, only a report is read, what is available to everyone in the open file. In another case, three appeals were identified at three court dates. A total of dozens of charges were raised in this case. Ladies and gentlemen! The last change, which I would like to point out - of course there are many more of them, but I have chosen only the most imaginative situations, cases where we have found the gross chronicity of proceedings due to the 19th century rules, it must be said directly - concerns the prohibition of judgment to the detriment. We now have the so-called. the bans of ne peius, formulated in such a way that the defendant against whom the proceedings at first instance were conditionally dismissed may not be convicted at an appeal court. In the proposed amendment, we provide for such a possibility of conviction in a situation where - I want to make it clear - the guilt and the culpability of such a defendant have been recognised by the Court of First Instance. After all, parole is nothing more than recognition of the defendant's guilt and culpability. So why create this kind of fiction, rescind the sentence to rediscover and cause another long-term delay in carrying out further actions? And since it's been many years... Please, we have one of the cases in which the sentence is conditionally remitted due to the inability of conviction. The procedure increased by one year and six months. In the following case, which has been pending since 2013, the judgment of the conditionally remitting procedure has been revoked. The case is still going on. High Izbo! Ladies and gentlemen! They were prepared by prominent judges, and I would like to say this straight away, judges who came to us to the ministry, often judges from common courts, regional courts, district courts, appeals, who still ruled yesterday or in the morning, and in the evening they were in the ministry, who knew their work and needed an efficient tool to bring justice. The proof of this is, for example, what we point out in the OSR, we have received such information that a real acceleration of the procedure, if our changes were already in force, would take place with 50% effectiveness. Today, processes are many times longer by 100% than they'd be after our shifts. In view of the above, I ask the High House to support the project. ParlaMint-PL_2019-03-13-sejm-78-1.u392	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! I am honored on behalf of the club of the Polish People's Party - Union of European Democrats to present our position on the government draft law amending the Code of Criminal Procedure and some other laws, print No. 3251. The aim of the project under consideration is to improve and accelerate criminal proceedings, to eliminate the perceived shortcomings and legislative errors that occur in the procedural law and to adapt procedural regulations to the challenges of technological development. The draft assumes that judges will not announce judgments and resolutions at a sitting that no one will attend. The draft also states that judges will not have to mention all the protocols and documents that are evidence at the hearings. The new provision assumes that, once the judicial duct is closed, all protocols and documents of evidence shall be deemed to have been disclosed without reading. The proposed reform of the Code of Criminal Procedure is also intended to put an end to the combinations with medical dismissals, which result in increased proceedings in courts. It's a common method of judicial obstruction today. After the reform, this activity is to be significantly reduced. Regardless of the reason for the absence of the parties, witnesses may be heard and other evidence may be provided for the hearing. The project also assumes that the evidence request (e.g. the document, the appointment of a witness) submitted after the deadline will in principle be rejected. The exception is only if the evidence determines the criminal responsibility of the perpetrator, which is the consequence of the primacy of the principle of material truth in the trial. This solution is also intended to reduce the obstruction of proceedings by continuous reporting after the deadline of the evidence requests. The proposed amendments also provide for the withdrawal of unnecessary hearings. As the MS pointed out, it is now a serious burden for the police and prosecutors in investigations and investigations to question all victims in cases involving hundreds and even thousands of people. This is about online fraud, financial pyramids, loan sharks, and the sale of a whole series of defective goods. Resignation of the obligation to interrogate all victims is intended to improve and speed up preparatory proceedings and to prevent unnecessary interrogation of victims. The draft also assumes that the court will be able to refrain from directly questioning witnesses of whom it is known that they will not bring anything relevant to the case. It goes on for example. a situation where the witness can at most confirm circumstances that are not questionable and have been confirmed. Then it is enough to read at the hearing the testimony of such a witness before the prosecutor - explained MS. The proposal also provides for a reduction in the appointment of new witnesses or documentary evidence at the stage of the appeal. However, this will be acceptable if the appellant could not request evidence before the court of first instance or if the fact he wanted to prove could not be the subject of the original proceedings. At the same time, the reform strengthens the role of appeals courts. They'll be able to convict the defendant who was found guilty by the court of first instance, but conditionally dismissed the case. The appeal court will also be able to tighten the sentence itself by serving life imprisonment. At present, the highest penalty a court of appeal can impose is 25 years of imprisonment, and if it considers a life sentence to be justified, it can only refer the case to a review by the court of first instance. The proposed novel also provides that anyone will be able to indicate a post office box for the effective delivery of pleadings (e.g. The court, the prosecutor, the police). In addition, the changes are intended to ensure that people with disabilities and obsessively ill will no longer have problems receiving pleadings. The new rules will allow them to authorise any person selected to receive correspondence at the post office. This solution removes barriers to access to justice and improves proceedings. Furthermore, the proposal assumes that the reasons for the judgments will be drawn up on a special form, in accordance with strict rules. Thus, according to the project's authors, they will become understandable and transparent. High Izbo! The information provided in the explanatory memorandum of the project shows that the proposed amendments are intended to improve, simplify and speed up court proceedings. This request is, of course, valid. We all know cases of judicial obstruction and over any measure of extended proceedings. However, we believe that changes in the organisation of the work of judges and courts are more important than broad legislative changes in order to speed up and improve criminal proceedings. There is a need to increase the number of posts, to introduce the practice of court planning or to refer certain cases to courts, administrative authorities. This is the problem of the actual ability of judges and their assistants to perform their duties. If there are simply judges missing in many courts, there are no assistants, there is no efficient management in the exchange of governors or directors, it is not possible to recognize the same number of cases in a given time unit. You can't just do that! It should also be noted that, to a large extent, the proposed amendments have already been introduced into the Code, only the current governing team has withdrawn them. After all, on 27 September 2013, a major amendment to the Code of Criminal Procedure was adopted, which entered into force on 1 July 2015. It was a thoughtful amendment, consulted for several months, developed in the environment of prosecutors, judges, lawyers. It was an amendment introduced under the motto of increasing the adversariality of the criminal process and contained many solutions to improve criminal proceedings. However, after the change of authorities on 11 March 2016, with effect from 15 April this year, a number of these amendments were withdrawn from the Code and returned to the original model of criminal proceedings. The changes were withdrawn in an unthinking, even accidental manner, before the 2013 reform could work for good. In the meantime, some of these changes that we can see in the current project were there. The authors of this project are completely inconsistent, because they are now restoring a large part of the solutions that they eliminated in the amendment two years ago. This means chaos and changing the procedure every now and then, without respect for the value of the sustainability and stability of the law. The question should also be asked why the Ministry of Justice has led to the removal of the article. 59a of the Penal Code. The recipe worked well, allowing him to stop prosecuting the perpetrator if he was reconciled to the victim. There are also positive elements in the project, e.g. the possibility of using information technology more widely in the delivery of procedural documents, and this should be commended.
ParlaMint-PL_2019-03-13-sejm-78-1.u393	A request was made in the discussion to reject the draft law at first reading. We will proceed to the vote on this proposal in the voting block. I'm calling a five-minute break. [[Adjournment from 10 a.m. 21 mins 12 h 21 min 22]] I'm reopening the session. We proceed with the examination of item 14. of the agenda: First reading of the government draft law amending the Act on Tax on Goods and Services and the Tax Order (Print No 3255). I ask the Undersecretary of State at the Ministry of Finance of Mr Filip Świtała to present the justification for the bill.
ParlaMint-PL_2019-03-13-sejm-78-1.u394	Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! The priority in recent years was to seal the tax system, especially as regards VAT, and improve VAT collection. Now it is time for the VAT system to simplify, improve the conditions for taxable persons to do business, give them more certainty. The current VAT system is complicated. It has too many entries in the annexes containing lists of goods subject to reduced rates. It contains numerous exemptions from the current Polish Classification of Products and Services. There is also a lack of certainty in this system, as there is no binding force for classification opinions issued by the Central Statistical Office in the field of classification of goods and services. Mr. Marshal! High Izbo! We have prepared a draft law on amending the Act on VAT on goods and services and the Act Tax Order, which will simplify the system of VAT rates, will ensure simplicity, transparency and the friendly application of the provisions of the VAT Act on rates. Firstly, the proposal provides for a change in the way goods and services are identified for VAT purposes. We are moving away from the current Polish Classification of Products and Services, PKWiU 2008, to the Combined Nomenclature, a system commonly used in the world and in the European Union. In the field of services we move to the current Polish Classification of Products and Services, i.e. PKWiU 2015. Secondly, the proposal provides for the introduction of a new matrix of VAT rates, constructed on the basis of taxation at the same rate, where possible, of whole groups of goods and services, and for the adoption of a general rule of reduction by way of a downward equation. This new matrix will arrange and significantly reduce the number of items in the new annexes containing exceptions, but will not significantly reduce the number of those goods which are subject to a reduced rate of taxation. This will therefore be a simplification. Importantly, the new annexes will include goods and services subject to reduced VAT rates, which are currently listed in the Implementing Regulation. The new rules will be much simpler and easier to apply. Thanks to the new matrix, similar goods will be taxed at the same rate. This will eliminate existing inconsistencies and irrationality at stakes. For example, at different rates, fruit is taxed - that's 5% and 8% , books - 5% and 23% when it comes to bakery products, we have almost the full range - 5%, 8% And 23%. The new VAT matrix will also eliminate quite often criticised arbitrary criteria determining the amount of the rate, e.g. date of minimum durability or date of shelf-life or water content in waffles. Thirdly, we are planning to introduce binding joint information, i.e. an instrument that will allow the company to know what VAT rate it is to apply and will provide it with certainty as to the application of that rate. Thanks to the new matrix, the VAT rate will be reduced to the following goods: bread, where we will move from these three rates to the lowest rate, pastry products, soups, broths, homogeneous foods, where we will also move from 8% to 5%. This will also include diet foods for special medical purposes, certain infant and infant products such as baby foods for infants and young children, pacifiers, diapers, car seats - here the rate will fall from 8% to 5%. Hygienic articles such as pads, hygienic tampons, diapers - from 8% to 5%. From 1 June 2019 we intend to reduce the rates to so-called. e-books and e-mails, including on audio books downloaded via the Internet, will be reduced from 23% to 5%, including books not marked with ISBN symbol and newspapers and magazines not marked with ISSN symbols. I would like to stress that the changes we propose in this law are not fiscal. They aim at simplifying the system and reducing certain existing inconsistencies. However, due to the proposed reductions, it is necessary to maintain a certain balance and to increase the rates at a point in time for certain goods. And so on. there will be increased rates for certain seafood such as lobsters and octopuses, crustaceans, molluscs and aquatic invertebrates, crabs, langus, shrimps, oysters, mussels, snails, and caviar. Here, the stakes are going to rise from 5% to 23%. This will also include meals sold in various catering establishments, which are part of these products. Beverages with added fruit or vegetable juice if, generally speaking, this addition does not exceed 20% - here the rate will rise from 5% to 23%. On the other hand, we'll keep the 5% stake on the so-called. 100-percent juices and so-called. nectars, or juices, with more real juice or real vegetable purée. The stake on ice used for food purposes will rise from 8 to 23%. There will also be an increase in the rate for specialist journals, except for regional and local journals, for which we will maintain the rate of 5%. As so far, goods and services normally used for agricultural production, such as farm animals, seeds, fertilisers, and agricultural machinery strings, will be taxed at an 8% rate. At the unchanged level will remain covered by the 8% rate of living services related to culture, sport, recreation, passenger transport, maintenance works or catering services. In view of the doubts that arise, I would like to make it clear that there will be no increase in the rate for water treatment and water supply services for residents through water supply networks and for the supply of water outside this system, namely barrels or tanks, we will remain here at an rate of 8%. One of the key elements, which I would like to stress, which significantly changes the Polish tax system, is the introduction of new binding joint information. This is an instrument that will provide certainty about classification. Today we have such a situation that we can apply to the CSO for statistical classification of the goods and then with this obtained classification go after the so-called. tax interpretation. This is a bad system because these GUS interpretations are changing. They are prepared for purposes other than tax purposes. We want to introduce binding tax information issued by tax authorities, in this case by national tax information authorities. An important feature of this new instrument will be that it will be issued in the form of a decision, i.e. it will be inviolable. At the same time, it will protect the application of the rate set in it not only of the direct addressee, i.e. the one who sought this binding information, but also of its counterparties, buyers throughout the chain of purchase and sale through which the goods are going. On the other hand, if such a situation occurs, the Authority will change the previously issued joint interpretation, e.g. finds that he was wrong that the interpretation was wrong, that the protection of the taxpayer will be preserved until the end of the next trading period. For example, if someone has a monthly settlement period and received binding joint information in January, he will be able to apply the rate resulting from this previous information by the end of February. On the other hand, if someone settles quarterly, because such situations also occur, in the event of receiving a binding interpretation in January he will be able to benefit until June, which is until the next accounting period, the next quarter. In addition to protection, binding stand information also gives entrepreneurs an additional advantage. With this binding information, the taxpayer will be able to manage its risks. He will be able to check whether the goods in question are subject to a reverse charge, whether the so-called "reverse charge' applies to them. the institution of joint and several liability. He will simply be able to ask whether what he buys is covered by these specific regulations. It will also be able to determine whether it sells goods which entitles to special exemptions, e.g. the application of an individual exemption. We anticipate that these new rates should start to apply from 1 January 2020. We want the provisions of the Act on which we are proceeding, the High Sejm, to be able to recommend, in a timely and efficient manner, that taxpayers have time between the entry into force of the Act and the application of these new rates from 1 January 2020 for preparation and application of binding joint information, where they will not be sure. We anticipate that, during the transitional period between the publication of the Act and the start of the application of the new rates, binding joint information will be sought, i.e. decisions on the rates that we will apply in 2020. Such a solution will enable companies to prepare for these new rates, to prepare their IT systems and to resolve any doubts. In addition, the project introduces minor changes in the scope of so-called. a flat-rate tax for farmers. This system will cover all products derived from the agricultural activity of a flat-rate farmer. This currently applies only to goods listed in Annex 2 of the VAT Act. In this respect, we will align the criteria with personal income tax. In the bill submitted, we have also translated the references contained in the provisions to the current PKWiU 2008 into appropriate CN codes for goods and the corresponding PKWiU 2015 symbols for services. In principle, these changes do not affect the scope of the relevant regulations. We estimate that the financial impact of this law will be negative and will be minus 328 million PLN per year. In 2019, if the law were to enter into force in the middle of the year, it would be lower and would be minus 109 million. We will also bear the costs of introducing a system of binding joint information. This will require staff costs and the organisation of workplaces. In total, we estimate that over the next 10 years, i.e. in the years 2019 and 2029, these costs will amount to about PLN 288 million, almost PLN 289 million. In conclusion, I would like to emphasise once again that despite the point increases in VAT rates for certain goods, the planned solutions do not have a fiscal objective. Our aim is to maximise the simplification of the VAT rate system, to ensure simplicity and transparency. This is intended to contribute significantly to improving the business environment for taxpayers, including micro-entrepreneurs and small and medium-sized enterprises. We assume that the new matrix of VAT rates will facilitate the application of tax legislation and their correct application in economic practice. The proposed regulations on the introduction of binding stand information, which gives taxpayers protection, will ensure stability in the area of tax law application. This will reduce the costs of running a business. This is a good project. It ensures simplicity, transparency and friendship of the system. In view of the above, I kindly ask the High House to refer this proposal to further parliamentary work. [[Applause]]

The proposed law aims to maximise the simplification of the VAT rate system and to ensure its simplicity and transparency. It will definitely contribute to improving the business conditions of taxpayers, including micro-entrepreneurs and small and medium-sized enterprises. Polish VAT rules provide for the application of three rates: 23%, 8% and 0% rates intended for intra-Community transactions. Multi-annual practice of applying so-called. The PKWiU has shown that the system of VAT rates based on it in Poland is indeed extensive and complex, resulting in significant difficulties in carrying out business activities related to the application of the applicable tax rates. It must be added that the PKWiU is also based on the national reverse load system, so-called. reverse charge, or a flat-rate system for farmers, as the Minister said. For this reason, it is planned to change the way goods and services are identified for VAT purposes and to depart from the application of the Polish Classification of Products and Services of PKWiU 2008 to the Combined Nomenclature for goods and the current Polish Classification of Products and Services of PKWiU 2015 in the field of services. At the same time, the project introduces instruments that give taxpayers and tax authorities greater certainty as to the correct application of VAT rules, inter alia. as regards the rates of that tax. The project introduces a matrix of VAT rates on the basis of a radical simplification of this system of rates. The aim of the introduced flat-rate scheme for farmers is to support agriculture by compensating at least part of the expenditure related to investments and the purchase of inputs. In view of the need to harmonise the application of tax legislation by reference to the Individual Income Tax Act, it is proposed to include the definition of agricultural products of all goods derived from the agricultural activity of a flat-rate farmer. The proposed law introduces binding relief information, which will provide taxpayers and tax authorities with more certainty as to the correctness of the tax rates applied. The binding joint information shall be intended, inter alia, for the purposes of this Regulation. the protection of the taxable person using it for VAT purposes. It will be a protective instrument - binding on the tax authorities of the entity for which it was issued and for a specific commodity or service. The objective of this project, i.e. taxation of the various VAT rates of the entire so-called departments. the combined nomenclature, will facilitate the use of tax legislation and their correct application in practice in the economy and reduce the time and costs of carrying out certain administrative activities. Regulation on the introduction of binding stand information giving specific protection to taxable persons will ensure stability in the area of tax law, which will reduce the costs of doing business. The proposed changes will have a similar effect in the flat-rate scheme for farmers. It will not only simplify the application of regulation in this area but also adapt the system to the current realities of economic life. The planned changes are not, as the Minister said, fiscal, but are intended to simplify the system and to significantly reduce the inconsistencies in the application of VAT rates. Simplification of tax rates should give an impetus to development.
